  Body: `encrypt()` adds an entry to `pending` and then calls `worker.postMessage(...)` without guarding for synchronous failures (e.g., worker already terminated, DataCloneError). If `postMessage` throws, the returned Promise never resolves/rejects and the `pending` map leaks the entry. Wrap `postMessage` in try/catch, and on error delete the pending entry and reject the Promise (optionally also reset/terminate the worker instance).
  DB ID: 3012006761


--- Thread PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53yQsd ---
  Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
  File: apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts:140
  Body: The batch path reads *all* `newFiles` into memory (`await file.arrayBuffer()` loop) before calling `client.uploadFiles(...)`. For large batches this can allocate O(N) plaintext buffers at once and defeats the intended “pipeline-style / bounded by UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY” memory behavior. Consider changing the batch API to accept `File` objects (or a per-file `read()` callback) so data is read inside the SDK’s p-limit pool, or otherwise limit/read sequentially before enqueueing each item into the pool

</details>

**Wrap Worker initialization and postMessage in try-catch to clean up pending entries on failure.**

If `getWorker()` or `postMessage()` throws synchronously, the `pending` map retains an orphaned entry that never resolves, causing memory leak. Always clean up the entry on sync errors.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
  encrypt(params: Parameters<ExternalEncryptFn>[0]): ReturnType<ExternalEncryptFn> {
    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      this.idCounter += 1;
      const id = `enc-${this.idCounter}-${Date.now()}`;
      this.pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
-
-      const worker = this.getWorker();
-      const message: EncryptRequest = {
-        type: 'encrypt',
-        id,
-        data: params.data,
-        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
-        encryptionMode: params.encryptionMode,
-      };
-
-      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty).
-      // If data is a subview, copy first — transferring .buffer would detach
-      // the entire underlying ArrayBuffer beyond the intended range.
-      const isFullBuffer =
-        params.data.byteOffset === 0 &&
-        params.data.byteLength === params.data.buffer.byteLength;
-      const transferData = isFullBuffer ? params.data : params.data.slice();
-      message.data = transferData;
-      worker.postMessage(message, [transferData.buffer] as Transferable[]);
+      try {
+        const worker = this.getWorker();
+        const message: EncryptRequest = {
+          type: 'encrypt',
+          id,
+          data: params.data,
+          userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
+          encryptionMode: params.encryptionMode,
+        };
+
+        // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty).
+        // If data is a subview, copy first — transferring .buffer would detach
+        // the entire underlying ArrayBuffer beyond the intended range.
+        const isFullBuffer =
+          params.data.byteOffset === 0 &&
+          params.data.byteLength === params.data.buffer.byteLength;
+        const transferData = isFullBuffer ? params.data : params.data.slice();
+        message.data = transferData;
+        worker.postMessage(message, [transferData.buffer] as Transferable[]);
+      } catch (error) {
+        this.pending.delete(id);
+        reject(error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error)));
+      }
    });
  }

  Body:
`encrypt()` adds an entry to `pending` and then calls `worker.postMessage(...)` without guarding for synchronous failures (e.g., worker already terminated, DataCloneError). If `postMessage` throws, the returned Promise never resolves/rejects and the `pending` map leaks the entry. Wrap `postMessage` in try/catch, and on error delete the pending entry and reject the Promise (optionally also reset/terminate the worker instance).

  Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
  File: apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts:140
  DB ID: 3012006806
  Body:
The batch path reads *all* `newFiles` into memory (`await file.arrayBuffer()` loop) before calling `client.uploadFiles(...)`. For large batches this can allocate O(N) plaintext buffers at once and defeats the intended “pipeline-style / bounded by UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY” memory behavior. Consider changing the batch API to accept `File` objects (or a per-file `read()` callback) so data is read inside the SDK’s p-limit pool, or otherwise limit/read sequentially before enqueueing each item into the pool.

  Body:
The method doc comment says memory is bounded by `UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY`, but `uploadFiles()` accepts `Uint8Array` buffers for every file; callers can still hold many large buffers in memory at once (as the web drop handler currently does). Consider rewording this to clarify it only bounds *in-flight ciphertext/encryption work*, or adjust the API to support lazy reading so end-to-end memory truly stays bounded.
```suggestion
   * Pipeline-style: each concurrency slot does encrypt -> pin -> free, so
   * in-flight ciphertext and encryption/pinning work are bounded by
   * UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY. Overall memory usage can still include all
   * caller-provided file buffers in the `files` array; callers handling large
   * batches should stream or chunk inputs if end-to-end memory must be bounded.
```

1	import type { ExternalEncryptFn } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
2	import type { EncryptRequest, EncryptResponse } from '../workers/encrypt.types';
3	
4	/**
5	 * Main-thread wrapper for the encryption Web Worker.
6	 *
7	 * Creates one Worker instance lazily on first use and reuses it for all
8	 * encryption operations. Each encrypt() call sends a message with a
9	 * correlation ID and returns a Promise resolved by the matching response.
10	 *
11	 * Uses Transferable ArrayBuffer transfers in both directions to avoid
12	 * copying large file data between threads.
13	 */
14	export class EncryptionWorkerService {
15	  private worker: Worker | null = null;
16	  private pending = new Map<
17	    string,
18	    {
19	      resolve: (value: Awaited<ReturnType<ExternalEncryptFn>>) => void;
20	      reject: (reason: Error) => void;
21	    }
22	  >();
23	  private idCounter = 0;
24	
25	  /** Lazily create the Worker on first use. */
26	  private getWorker(): Worker {
27	    if (!this.worker) {
28	      // Static string literal required for Vite static analysis
29	      this.worker = new Worker(new URL('../workers/encrypt.worker.ts', import.meta.url), {
30	        type: 'module',
31	      });
32	      this.worker.onmessage = (event: MessageEvent<EncryptResponse>) => {
33	        const response = event.data;
34	        const entry = this.pending.get(response.id);
35	        if (!entry) return;
36	        this.pending.delete(response.id);
37	
38	        if (response.type === 'error') {
39	          entry.reject(new Error(response.error));
40	        } else {
41	          entry.resolve({
42	            ciphertext: response.ciphertext,
43	            wrappedKey: response.wrappedKey,
44	            iv: response.iv,
45	            fileKey: response.fileKey,
46	            originalSize: response.originalSize,
47	            encryptedSize: response.encryptedSize,
48	          });
49	        }
50	      };
51	      this.worker.onerror = (event) => {
52	        // Reject all pending operations on worker crash
53	        for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
54	          entry.reject(new Error(`Worker error: ${event.message}`));
55	        }
56	        this.pending.clear();
57	      };
58	    }
59	    return this.worker;
60	  }
61	
62	  /**
63	   * Encrypt file data in the Web Worker.
64	   *
65	   * Transfers the data ArrayBuffer to the Worker (zero-copy) and receives
66	   * the ciphertext ArrayBuffer back (zero-copy). After calling this, the
67	   * original `data` Uint8Array becomes zero-length (transferred).
68	   */
69	  encrypt(params: Parameters<ExternalEncryptFn>[0]): ReturnType<ExternalEncryptFn> {
70	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
71	      this.idCounter += 1;
72	      const id = `enc-${this.idCounter}-${Date.now()}`;
73	      this.pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
74	
75	      const worker = this.getWorker();
76	      const message: EncryptRequest = {
77	        type: 'encrypt',
78	        id,
79	        data: params.data,
80	        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
81	        encryptionMode: params.encryptionMode,
82	      };
83	
84	      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty).
85	      // If data is a subview, copy first — transferring .buffer would detach
86	      // the entire underlying ArrayBuffer beyond the intended range.
87	      const isFullBuffer =
88	        params.data.byteOffset === 0 && params.data.byteLength === params.data.buffer.byteLength;
89	      const transferData = isFullBuffer ? params.data : params.data.slice();
90	      message.data = transferData;
91	      worker.postMessage(message, [transferData.buffer] as Transferable[]);
92	    });
93	  }
94	
95	  /**
96	   * Build an ExternalEncryptFn compatible with sdkCore.uploadFile's encryptFn param.
97	   * This is the function passed to client.uploadFiles({ encryptFn }).
98	   */
99	  createEncryptFn(): ExternalEncryptFn {
100	    return (params) => this.encrypt(params);
101	  }
102	
103	  /**
104	   * Terminate the Worker and reject all pending operations.
105	   * Call on logout or SDK client destroy.
106	   */
107	  destroy(): void {
108	    if (this.worker) {
109	      this.worker.terminate();
110	      this.worker = null;
111	    }
112	    for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
113	      entry.reject(new Error('EncryptionWorkerService destroyed'));
114	    }
115	    this.pending.clear();
116	  }
117	}
118	
119	/** Singleton instance. Created lazily, destroyed on logout. */
120	let instance: EncryptionWorkerService | null = null;
121	
122	export function getEncryptionWorker(): EncryptionWorkerService {
123	  if (!instance) {
124	    instance = new EncryptionWorkerService();
125	  }
126	  return instance;
127	}
128	
129	export function destroyEncryptionWorker(): void {
130	  if (instance) {
131	    instance.destroy();
132	    instance = null;
133	  }
134	}
135

commit 6a6939b2a71a922cb79f7a537bd548fcf1b2e6d1
Author: Michael Yankelev <myankelev@gmail.com>
Date:   Mon Mar 30 19:44:32 2026 +0200

    docs(37): track deferred items from PR #416 review
    
    Add 3 deferred ideas to CONTEXT.md and VERIFICATION.md:
    - Batch upload secondary pin warning events (BYO-IPFS)
    - AbortSignal support for in-flight batch cancellation
    - Lazy file reading within p-limit concurrency pool
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 5d2ad59d88d7

diff --git a/.planning/phases/37-parallel-batch-upload-pipeline/37-CONTEXT.md b/.planning/phases/37-parallel-batch-upload-pipeline/37-CONTEXT.md
index 48c9d3e78..897c20889 100644
--- a/.planning/phases/37-parallel-batch-upload-pipeline/37-CONTEXT.md
+++ b/.planning/phases/37-parallel-batch-upload-pipeline/37-CONTEXT.md
@@ -129,6 +129,9 @@ Scope: TypeScript SDK (`@cipherbox/sdk`, `@cipherbox/sdk-core`) and web app (`ap
 - **Adaptive concurrency based on file size** — More concurrent slots for small files, fewer for large. Implement when fixed pool of 3 proves insufficient.
 - **FUSE write-coalescing for desktop** — Buffer all `release()` calls within a time window, then batch-publish folder metadata once. Requires FUSE architecture changes.
 - **Accumulated retry batching** — If many files fail and are retried, batch the retries into a single folder publish instead of N individual publishes. Implement if failure rates warrant it.
+- **Batch upload secondary pin warning events** — BYO-IPFS `pinFn` wrapper in `uploadFiles()` discards `secondaryWarning` from `pinWithMode()`, so `pin:secondaryFailed` events are never emitted for batch uploads in dual-pin mode. Needs per-file warning capture and a batch equivalent event. (PR #416 review — Copilot thread 3)
+- **AbortSignal support for in-flight batch uploads** — Once `client.uploadFiles()` is invoked, there is no way to cancel in-flight or not-yet-started uploads from the UI. Requires adding an `AbortSignal` parameter to `uploadFiles()` and propagating it through p-limit and `sdkCore.uploadFile()`. The pre-upload cancel check (before `arrayBuffer()`) is the only cancellation point today. (PR #416 review — Copilot thread 6, CodeRabbit thread 9)
+- **Lazy file reading within concurrency pool** — `useDropUpload` reads all file buffers into `Uint8Array` upfront before calling `uploadFiles()`. For large batches this allocates all memory simultaneously instead of pipelining reads through the p-limit pool. Fix requires changing the SDK contract to accept `File` objects (or a read callback) instead of `Uint8Array`. Bounded today by MAX_FILE_SIZE=100MB. (PR #416 review — CodeRabbit thread 8)
 
 ### Reviewed Todos (not folded)
```

## Comprehensive Deferred Items & Future Work Report: Phases 28-37

Based on my thorough exploration of all context, discussion logs, and verification documents for phases 28 through 37, here is a complete inventory of deferred items, out-of-scope decisions, and items marked as not implemented:

### **PHASE 29: Infrastructure Hardening**

#### Deferred Ideas

   - Future scope: Production Kubo deployment hardening when production environment exists

   - Status: Future milestone (not current milestone)
   - Reason: Only relevant when production environment is established

3. **Periodic reconciliation job to catch unenrollment failures**
   - Status: Could be added if fire-and-forget proves insufficient
   - Reason: Fire-and-forget pattern is current design choice; fallback available if needed

---

### **PHASE 30: Web App Observability**

   - Status: Stays dev-only per Phase 22 decision
   - Reason: Performance instrumentation is development tool, not production metric

   - Reason: Core SDK migration is priority; testing can follow

3. **Desktop app adoption of new SDK exports**
   - Status: Future milestone
   - Reason: Phase 31 prepares SDK layer; desktop integration deferred to later milestone

4. **`useFileOperations.ts` decomposition**
   - Status: Not in scope for Phase 31
   - Reason: Not in the 900+ line targets; separate decomposition effort

---

### **PHASE 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution**

#### Deferred Ideas
1. **Windows WinFsp async FilePointer resolution**
   - Status: Follow-up phase (Phase 33)
   - Reason: Same blocking pattern exists in Windows code; phase-specific (requires Windows testing machine)
   - Can execute in parallel: Platform-specific code paths don't overlap

2. **Batch IPNS resolve API endpoint**
   - Status: Future performance optimization
   - Reason: Could reduce round trips for folders with many files; not needed for Phase 32

#### Known Limitations
- Windows drain calls were added but full poll-wait logic deferred to Phase 33

### **PHASE 33: Windows Async FilePointer Resolution**

#### Deferred Ideas
- **None** — phase is tightly scoped as a direct port of Phase 32 to Windows

   - Reason: Phase 36 is UI-only refactor; encryption performance optimization is Phase 37 scope

### **PHASE 37: Parallel Batch Upload Pipeline**

#### Folded Todos (Incorporated into Phase Scope)
1. **"Offload large file encryption to Web Worker"** (from 2026-02-07-web-worker-large-file-encryption.md)
   - Status: Folded into Phase 37 scope (D-07/D-08)
   - Reason: Web Workers enable true parallelism; natural fit while redesigning pipeline

#### Deferred Ideas
1. **Adaptive concurrency based on file size**
   - Status: Future optimization when fixed pool of 3 proves insufficient
   - Reason: Fixed pool of 3 is simple and tunable; adaptive sizing adds complexity
   - Current: Concurrency pool size is named constant for easy future tuning

2. **FUSE write-coalescing for desktop batch upload optimization**
   - Status: Requires separate phase for FUSE architecture changes
   - Reason: Desktop uploads arrive one-at-a-time through `release()` callbacks; FUSE layer has no batch context
   - Impact: Rust SDK `upload_files()` method would exist but can't be naturally used by FUSE without coalescing layer

3. **Accumulated retry batching**
   - Status: Implement if failure rates warrant it
   - Reason: If many files fail, batch the retries into single folder publish instead of N individual publishes
   - Current: Failed file retry uses existing single-file `uploadFile()` method

4. **Batch upload secondary pin warning events**
   - Status: Deferred (noted from PR #416 review, Copilot thread 3)
   - Reason: BYO-IPFS `pinFn` wrapper in `uploadFiles()` discards `secondaryWarning` from `pinWithMode()`
   - Impact: `pin:secondaryFailed` events are never emitted for batch uploads in dual-pin mode
   - Future: Needs per-file warning capture and batch equivalent event

5. **AbortSignal support for in-flight batch uploads**
   - Status: Deferred (noted from PR #416 review, Copilot thread 6, CodeRabbit thread 9)
   - Reason: Once `client.uploadFiles()` is invoked, no way to cancel in-flight or not-yet-started uploads from UI
   - Impact: Medium priority — cancel button is no-op once SDK call starts
   - Solution: Requires adding `AbortSignal` parameter to `uploadFiles()` and propagating through p-limit and `sdkCore.uploadFile()`

6. **Lazy file reading within concurrency pool**
   - Status: Deferred (noted from PR #416 review, CodeRabbit thread 8)
   - Reason: `useDropUpload` reads all file buffers into `Uint8Array` upfront before calling `uploadFiles()`
   - Impact: For large batches, allocates all memory simultaneously instead of pipelining reads through p-limit pool
   - Medium priority — memory spike on large batches (bounded by 100MB MAX_FILE_SIZE)
   - Solution: Change SDK contract to accept `File` objects (or read callback) instead of `Uint8Array`

---

Perfect! Now I have all the information. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

## CipherBox Filesystem Specification - Complete Research Report

Based on my thorough investigation of the CipherBox codebase, here is a comprehensive document of the filesystem rules and constraints:

### 1. FILE/FOLDER NAMING RULES

**Name Format:**
- Names are stored as plaintext UTF-8 strings (encrypted alongside metadata)
- No length restrictions documented at the application layer
- Names stored in FolderChild type (FolderEntry.name, FilePointer.name)
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/core/src/folder/types.ts` lines 35-36, 61-62

**Case Sensitivity:**
- **macOS/Linux (FUSE)**: Case-sensitive storage and matching
  - Unicode normalization to NFC (composed form) applied for consistent lookups
  - Handles both NFC and NFD forms that macOS may send
  - Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/inode.rs` lines 18-34
  
- **Windows (WinFsp)**: Case-insensitive filesystem
  - Names normalized to lowercase for HashMap key matching in user-mode filesystem
  - Original casing preserved in InodeData.name for display
  - Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/inode.rs` lines 36-39

- **Web/API layer**: Case-sensitive exact match for duplicate detection
  - Uses strict equality (`===`) checks
  - Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts` lines 249, 365, 535, 753, 831, 895
  - Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/folder/index.ts` lines 249

### 2. RESERVED/SPECIAL NAMES

**Platform-Specific Special Files (Never created, synced, or displayed):**

Cross-platform files filtered in FUSE:
- Files starting with `._` (macOS resource forks)
- `.DS_Store`, `.Trashes`, `.fseventsd`, `.Spotlight-V100`
- `.hidden`, `.localized`, `.metadata_never_index` and variants
- `.ql_disablecache`, `.ql_disablethumbnails`
- `DCIM` (Digital Camera Images directory)
- `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini` (Windows)
- `.directory` (KDE), `.Trash-*` (Linux per-user trash), `.gvfs`, `.xdg-volume-info`

Windows-specific additional filters:
- `$recycle.bin`, `System Volume Information`, `Recycler`
- Files starting with `$` (alternate data streams)
- Files containing `:Zone.Identifier`
- `pagefile.sys`, `swapfile.sys`, `hiberfil.sys`

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/helpers.rs` lines 10-55

Implementation: These names are rejected during file creation (EACCES) and hidden from directory listings:
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` lines 141, 415
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/dir_ops.rs` line 113
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` line 141

No explicit Windows reserved device names (CON, PRN, NUL, etc.) validation found in CipherBox code.

### 3. CHARACTER RESTRICTIONS

**Forbidden Characters:**
- Null bytes (0x00): UTF-8 validation required by `to_str()` conversions in FUSE callback handlers
- Path separators: Not explicitly validated; stored in flat folder structure

**Allowed Characters:**
- Full UTF-8 range (excluding null bytes)
- All Unicode characters supported including accented letters, emojis
- No explicit regex validation found for character restrictions

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` lines 133-139 (UTF-8 validation on name.to_str())

### 4. FILE SIZE LIMITS

**Maximum File Size: 100 MB**
- Client-side limit: `MAX_FILE_SIZE = 100 * 1024 * 1024` (100MB)
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` line 13
- Enforced in upload form validation and oversized file filtering (line 41)
- Per FILE-01 requirement in roadmap

**Server-side validation (API):**
- Same 100MB limit enforced at `/ipfs/upload` endpoint with MaxFileSizeValidator
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts` lines 43, 91, 98
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipfs/dto/register-cid.dto.ts` lines 5, 19

### 5. STORAGE QUOTA LIMITS

**Total Storage Quota: 500 MiB (524,288,000 bytes)**
- Constant: `QUOTA_LIMIT_BYTES = 500 * 1024 * 1024`
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts` line 19
- Also in FUSE constants: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/constants.rs` line 9 as `QUOTA_BYTES`

**Quota Checking:**
- Enforced before file uploads via `quotaStore.canUpload(totalSize)` check
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` lines 49-55
- Quota is advisory-only for BYO-IPFS users (they manage their own storage)
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/vault/dto/quota.dto.ts` line 29

### 6. FOLDER DEPTH LIMITS

**Maximum Folder Nesting Depth: 20 levels**
- Constant: `MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH = 20`
- Multiple locations confirming same value:
  - `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts` line 48
  - `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/folder-helpers.ts` line 58
  - `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/components/file-browser/MoveDialog.tsx` line 1 (derived)

**Enforcement:**
- On folder creation: Checked against parent depth (line 290 in folder.service.ts)
- On folder move: Checked for dest_depth + 1 + subtree_depth > MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts` lines 756-760
- Error messages: "Cannot create folder: maximum depth of 20 exceeded"
- Per FOLD-03 requirement in roadmap

### 7. DUPLICATE NAME HANDLING

**Duplicate Detection: Case-sensitive, exact match**
- No files/folders with identical names allowed in the same parent folder
- Uses strict string equality (`===`) comparison

**Duplicate Check Implementation:**
```typescript
const nameExists = children.some((c) => c.name === params.newName && c.id !== params.childId);
if (nameExists) throw new Error('An item with this name already exists');
```

Locations checked:
- File upload (during registration): `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts` line 535-537
- Folder rename: line 365-366
- File rename: line 895-896
- Move operations: lines 753-754, 831-832
- SDK core folder operations: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/folder/index.ts` lines 249-250, 292-293, 336-339

**Batch upload duplicate handling:**
- Prevents duplicate names within a single upload batch
- Allows replace-in-place for files already in target folder
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` lines 58-88

### 8. FILE/FOLDER NAME COLLISION HANDLING

**File-to-Folder collision prevention:**
- Cannot upload a file with the same name as an existing folder
- Error: "Cannot upload file(s) with the same name as an existing folder"
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` lines 82-88

**Folder-to-File collision handling:**
- Not explicitly checked in folder creation
- Relies on general duplicate name detection

### 9. METADATA CONSTRAINTS

**File Metadata (FileMetadata):**
- MIME type: Required, stored as string
- File size: Stored as number (bytes) before encryption
- CID (Content Identifier): IPFS CID string
- Encryption mode: 'GCM' or 'CTR' (defaults to 'GCM')
- File key: ECIES-wrapped AES-256 key (hex string)
- IV: Hex-encoded 12-byte initialization vector
- Timestamps: Unix milliseconds (createdAt, modifiedAt)
- Versions: Optional array of past file versions (max 10)

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/core/src/file/types.ts` lines 30-51

**Folder Metadata (FolderMetadata):**
- Version: Must be 'v2' (v1 rejected)
- Children: Array of FolderEntry (subfolders) or FilePointer (files)

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/core/src/folder/types.ts` lines 15-20

**Encryption:**
- Both file and folder metadata encrypted with AES-256-GCM
- IV stored as hex string, ciphertext as base64

### 10. FILE VERSIONING CONSTRAINTS

**Maximum Versions Per File: 10**
- Constant: `MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE = 10`
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/constants.rs` line 12

**Version Creation Cooldown: 15 minutes**
- Only creates new version if most recent version is older than 15 minutes (900,000 ms)
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/constants.rs` lines 14-16

### 11. FUSE/FILESYSTEM CONSTRAINTS

**Content Download Timeout: 120 seconds**
- Network timeout for file content fetch during filesystem operations
- Large files (up to 100MB) expected to take 30-60 seconds from staging IPFS
- Blocks NFS thread but content is cached after open()
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/constants.rs` lines 18-22

**FUSE Block Size: 4096 bytes**
- Standard page size for statfs reporting
- Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/inode.rs` line 46

### 12. NAME NORMALIZATION FOR FILESYSTEM

**macOS (FUSE):**
- Unicode NFC (composed form) normalization applied
- Handles both NFC and NFD forms from macOS NFS client
- Consistent lookups for accented characters

**Windows (WinFsp):**
- Lowercase normalization for case-insensitive matching
- Original case preserved in storage
- Example: Both "File.txt" and "file.txt" resolve to same file (lowercase key)

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/inode.rs` lines 27-40

### 13. EMOJI AND UNICODE SUPPORT

- Full UTF-8 support including emojis
- Unicode normalization (NFC on macOS, lowercase on Windows) applied at filesystem layer
- Stored as plaintext in encrypted metadata

### 14. UPLOAD VALIDATION

**Pre-Upload Checks:**
1. File size > 100MB: Rejected
2. Duplicate names in batch: Rejected
3. File-folder name collision: Rejected
4. Quota available: Checked (canUpload check)
5. Parent folder exists and is registered: Checked

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` lines 40-88

## CipherBox Feature State Research Report

Based on a thorough investigation of the codebase, here's the complete current feature state organized by category:

### 1. BATCH UPLOADS

**Status: FULLY IMPLEMENTED (Phase 37)**

**SDK Level (TypeScript @cipherbox/sdk):**
- ✅ **uploadFiles()** method exists at `/packages/sdk/src/client.ts:842`
  - Accepts array of files with concurrent upload support (UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY = 3)
  - Returns `{ successes: [{fileName, cid}], failures: [{fileName, error}] }`
  - Supports progress callbacks: `onFileProgress(fileName, percent)`, `onFileComplete(fileName)`, `onFileError(fileName, error)`
  - Supports external encryption function via `encryptFn` parameter
  - Supports external pinning function via `pinFn` parameter
  - Handles collision detection (same filename in concurrent uploads)
  - Single folder publish for entire batch (efficient)
  - Batch IPNS record publishing (concurrent with folder publish)

**Web Level:**
- ✅ **useDropUpload()** hook at `/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts`
  - Uses SDK's `uploadFiles()` for new files (lines 147-171)
  - Detects external file drags vs internal app drags
  - Enforces 100MB per-file limit
  - Checks quota before upload
  - Handles duplicate files separately (via old encrypt+upload path for Replace dialog staging)
  - Validates file-folder name collisions
  - No batch upload in desktop app currently

**Desktop:**
- ❌ No batch upload implementation in desktop app yet
- Desktop uses old single-file upload patterns

**Rust SDK:**
- ❌ No batch upload in Rust SDK (`crates/sdk/`)
- Rust SDK provides stateful orchestration: sync daemon, write queue, key state, device registry (no upload operations)

**Notes:**
- Phase 37-02 completed 2026-03-30: Web Worker encryption wired into batch upload pipeline
- Duplicate files handled separately (encrypted on old path, uploaded to IPFS for Replace dialog staging)

### 4. WEB WORKER ENCRYPTION

**Status: FULLY IMPLEMENTED (Phase 37-02)**

**Location:**
- Worker: `/apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts`
- Service: `/apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts`

**Features:**
- ✅ Off-main-thread file encryption using @cipherbox/crypto primitives
- ✅ Supports AES-GCM and AES-CTR modes (detected from file MIME/size)
- ✅ Transferable ArrayBuffer transfers (zero-copy in both directions)
- ✅ Singleton EncryptionWorkerService with lazy Worker creation
- ✅ Promise-based API with correlation IDs (`enc-{counter}-{timestamp}`)
- ✅ Multiplexed concurrent operations on single Worker
- ✅ Worker termination on logout via `destroyEncryptionWorker()`
- ✅ ExternalEncryptFn factory for SDK integration

**Integration:**
- Wired into SDK batch upload via `/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts:145`
- Used in client.uploadFiles() with `{ encryptFn }` parameter

**Notes:**
- Key material cleared in worker memory before postMessage
- Handles worker crashes gracefully (rejects all pending operations)
- Only used for new file batch uploads (duplicates use old single-file path)

1	# CipherBox
2	
3	## What This Is
4	
5	CipherBox is a production-grade, privacy-first encrypted cloud storage platform using IPFS/IPNS and Web3Auth. It provides zero-knowledge file storage with user-to-user sharing, link sharing, client-side search, multi-factor authentication, file versioning, conflict detection, recycle bin, and cross-platform desktop apps (macOS, Windows, Linux). The server is cryptographically unable to access user data.
6	
7	## Core Value
8	
9	**Zero-knowledge privacy**: Files are encrypted client-side before leaving the device, and encryption keys exist only in client memory. The server is cryptographically unable to access user data.
10	
11	## Current Milestone: v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
12	
13	**Goal:** Make CipherBox more IPFS-native — replace delegated-ipfs.dev, migrate selected vault state to IPFS/IPNS, add BYO-IPFS server-relay support, and establish performance baselines.
14	
15	**Target features:**
16	
17	- Reliable IPNS resolution (replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted or alternative provider)
18	- Reduce database dependence where feasible — migrate vault crypto material to IPFS while retaining `folder_ipns`, shares, device approvals, quota tracking, and the DB fallback for `encryptedRootFolderKey` in v1.1
19	- Bring-your-own IPFS node support via server-relay flow (client-direct deferred to v1.2)
20	- Comprehensive performance baselines (API, client, IPFS/IPNS latency, end-to-end user journeys)
21	
22	## Requirements
23	
24	### Validated (Milestone 1 — Staging MVP)
25	
26	- Web3Auth authentication (email, OAuth, magic link, external wallet) — v0.1
27	- Client-side AES-256-GCM encryption + ECIES key wrapping — v0.1
28	- IPFS file storage via Kubo with IPNS metadata — v0.1
29	- Full file/folder CRUD with 20-level hierarchy — v0.1
30	- File browser web UI with terminal aesthetic — v0.1
```

1	# Architecture
2	
3	CipherBox implements layered, zero-knowledge encryption where **all user data is encrypted client-side before leaving the device**. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not plaintext file sizes (encrypted blob sizes are visible for storage accounting).

1	//! Shared helper functions for the CipherBox FUSE filesystem.
2	//!
3	//! These functions are used by both macOS (fuser) and Windows (WinFsp)
4	//! filesystem implementations.
5	
6	/// Returns true if this filename is a platform-specific special file
7	/// that should never be created, synced, or shown in directory listings.
8	///
9	/// Covers macOS, Windows, and Linux special files.
10	pub fn is_platform_special(name: &str) -> bool {
11	    // macOS
12	    name.starts_with("._")
13	        || name == ".DS_Store"
14	        || name == ".Trashes"
15	        || name == ".fseventsd"
16	        || name == ".Spotlight-V100"
17	        || name == ".hidden"
18	        || name == ".localized"
19	        || name == ".metadata_never_index"
20	        || name == ".metadata_never_index_unless_rootfs"
21	        || name == ".metadata_direct_scope_only"
22	        || name == ".ql_disablecache"
23	        || name == ".ql_disablethumbnails"
24	        || name == "DCIM"
25	    // Windows
26	        || name == "Thumbs.db"
27	        || name == "desktop.ini"
28	    // Linux
29	        || name == ".directory"             // KDE directory metadata
30	        || name.starts_with(".Trash-")      // Per-user trash dirs (.Trash-1000)
31	        || name == ".gvfs"                  // GNOME Virtual File System
32	        || name == ".xdg-volume-info"       // XDG volume info
33	}
34	
35	/// Returns true if this filename is a Windows-specific special file
36	/// that should never be created, synced, or shown in directory listings.
37	///
38	/// This is a superset check for Windows: includes NTFS system files,
39	/// alternate data streams, and recycle bin artifacts in addition to
40	/// the cross-platform checks.
41	pub fn is_windows_special(name: &str) -> bool {
42	    let lower = name.to_lowercase();
43	    matches!(
44	        lower.as_str(),
45	        "desktop.ini"
46	            | "thumbs.db"
47	            | "$recycle.bin"
48	            | "system volume information"
49	            | "recycler"
50	            | "pagefile.sys"
51	            | "swapfile.sys"
52	            | "hiberfil.sys"
53	    ) || lower.contains(":zone.identifier")
54	        || lower.starts_with('$')
55	}
56	
57	/// Detect MIME type from file extension.
58	///
59	/// Delegates to the shared implementation in `cipherbox_crypto::utils`.
60	pub fn mime_from_extension(filename: &str) -> String {
61	    cipherbox_crypto::utils::mime_from_extension(filename).to_string()
62	}
63	
64	/// Build a human-readable breadcrumb path for a folder inode.
65	/// Walks parent_ino upward to root, concatenating names with " / ".
66	/// Example: "My Vault / Documents / Reports"
67	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
68	pub fn build_folder_path(fs: &crate::CipherBoxFS, folder_ino: u64) -> String {
69	    use crate::inode::InodeKind;
70	    let mut parts = Vec::new();
71	    let mut current = folder_ino;
72	    for _ in 0..20 { // Safety limit to prevent infinite loops
73	        match fs.inodes.get(current) {
74	            Some(inode) => {
75	                match &inode.kind {
76	                    InodeKind::Root { .. } => {
77	                        parts.push("My Vault".to_string());
78	                        break;
79	                    }
80	                    _ => {

1	//! Shared constants for the CipherBox FUSE filesystem.
2	//!
3	//! These constants are used by both macOS (fuser) and Windows (WinFsp)
4	//! filesystem implementations.
5	
6	use std::time::Duration;
7	
8	/// Total storage quota in bytes (500 MiB).
9	pub const QUOTA_BYTES: u64 = 500 * 1024 * 1024;
10	
11	/// Maximum number of past versions to keep per file.
12	pub const MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE: usize = 10;
13	
14	/// Cooldown period for desktop FUSE version creation (15 minutes in milliseconds).
15	/// Only creates a new version entry if the most recent version is older than this.
16	pub const VERSION_COOLDOWN_MS: u64 = 15 * 60 * 1000;
17	
18	/// Maximum time for file content download in open().
19	/// Large files (e.g., 64MB) can take 30-60s from staging IPFS.
20	/// This blocks the NFS thread, but since the content is cached after
21	/// open(), all subsequent reads are instant.
22	pub const CONTENT_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(120);
23

1	//! Inode table mapping inode numbers to folder/file metadata.
2	//!
3	//! The inode table is rebuilt on mount from IPNS metadata. Folders are loaded
4	//! lazily: children are populated on first readdir/lookup, not upfront.
5	//! Each folder inode stores its decrypted IPNS private key for write operations.
6	
7	#[cfg(feature = "fuse")]
8	use fuser::FileType;
9	
10	use std::collections::HashMap;
11	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
12	use std::time::{Duration, SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
13	
14	use zeroize::Zeroizing;
15	
16	use cipherbox_core::folder::{FolderChild, FolderMetadata};
17	
18	/// Normalize a filename to NFC (composed) form for consistent HashMap lookups.
19	/// macOS NFS client may send names in either NFC or NFD form; FUSE-T's go-nfsv4
20	/// may also re-normalize. By normalizing to NFC on both storage and lookup,
21	/// we avoid mismatches with accented characters (e.g., `e` vs `e` + combining grave).
22	///
23	/// On Windows, WinFsp sends callbacks with arbitrary casing (often uppercased)
24	/// for case-insensitive volumes. We fold to lowercase for consistent HashMap
25	/// key matching while preserving original casing in InodeData.name.
26	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
27	pub(crate) fn normalize_name(name: &str) -> String {
28	    // unicode-normalization is a dependency of the fuse feature.
29	    // On Windows (winfsp feature), fold to lowercase for case-insensitive matching.
30	    // WinFsp's case-insensitive lookup is the user-mode filesystem's responsibility.
31	    #[cfg(feature = "fuse")]
32	    {
33	        use unicode_normalization::UnicodeNormalization;
34	        name.nfc().collect()
35	    }
36	    #[cfg(all(feature = "winfsp", not(feature = "fuse")))]
37	    {
38	        name.to_lowercase()
39	    }
40	}
41	
42	/// Root inode number (standard FUSE convention).
43	pub const ROOT_INO: u64 = 1;
44	
45	/// Default block size for statfs reporting.
46	pub const BLOCK_SIZE: u32 = 4096;
47	
48	// -- FileAttrs ---------------------------------------------------------------
49	
50	/// Platform-agnostic file attributes.
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/README.md",
  "content": "<p align=\"center\">\n  <img src=\"./cipherbox logo.png\" alt=\"CipherBox Logo\" width=\"450\"/>\n</p>\n\n<h3 align=\"center\">Privacy-first encrypted cloud storage with decentralized persistence</h3>\n\n---\n\n## What is CipherBox\n\nCipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first cloud storage. All encryption happens client-side — the server never sees plaintext data, file names, or encryption keys. Files are stored on IPFS for decentralized persistence, keys are derived deterministically via Web3Auth so the same user always gets the same vault regardless of login method, and data can be exported for independent recovery without CipherBox.\n\n## Acknowledgements\n\nThis project is inspired by discussions and planning while working on [ChainSafe Files](https://github.com/chainsafe/ui-monorepo). A massive shout-out to all the colleagues I got to work with on the original ChainSafe Files project, who unknowingly contributed to this phoenix rising out of the ashes.\n\n## Features\n\n- **Authentication** — Web3Auth MPC Core Kit: email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, external wallet (SIWE). MFA via device factor with BIP39 recovery phrase.\n- **Encryption** — Client-side AES-256-GCM for files and metadata. ECIES secp256k1 key wrapping (per-file, per-folder random keys). AES-256-CTR streaming for large media files. Web Worker offloading for batch encryption.\n- **File Management** — Upload, download, rename, move, delete. Batch upload pipeline with concurrent encryption and pinning. Nested folders up to 20 levels. Drag-and-drop. File versioning with point-in-time restore (up to 10 versions per file).\n- **Sharing** — User-to-user sharing with ECIES re-wrapping (read-only and read-write). Invite-link sharing with time-limited access tokens. Multi-recipient sharing. Lazy key rotation on revoke.\n- **Recycle Bin** — 30-day soft-delete with restore and permanent delete.\n- **Search** — Client-side encrypted search index across file and folder names. Fuzzy matching with Cmd/Ctrl+K shortcut.\n- **Media Preview** — Image, PDF, video (streaming CTR decryption), and audio preview.\n- **Sync** — Multi-device via IPNS polling (~30s). Conflict detection and resolution (sequence-number based).\n- **Desktop** — macOS and Windows via Tauri v2 + FUSE-T/WinFsp virtual filesystem mount. Background sync with system tray. SMB backend on macOS.\n- **TEE Republishing** — Automatic IPNS record refresh every 6 hours via Phala Cloud. Zero-knowledge: keys decrypted only inside hardware enclaves.\n- **Data Portability** — Full vault export (JSON + encrypted blobs). Standalone recovery with private key — no CipherBox required.\n- **Observability** — Structured logging (web), Prometheus metrics endpoint (API), Grafana Faro integration, load testing infrastructure.\n- **BYO IPFS** — Bring-your-own IPFS node support via server-relay flow. Dual-pin mode with automatic migration.\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```text\n┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n│  Web / Desktop│────>│  CipherBox   │────>│  PostgreSQL   │\n│   (Client)   │     │    API       │     │  (Metadata)   │\n└──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘     └───────────────┘\n       │                    │\n       │ Encrypted          │ Relay\n       │ blobs              │\n       v                    v\n┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n│    IPFS      │     │  TEE Worker   │\n│  (Storage)   │     │ (IPNS Refresh)│\n└──────────────┘     └───────────────┘\n```\n\nThe client encrypts everything locally. The API is a zero-knowledge relay — it stores encrypted keys and routes IPFS/IPNS operations but never accesses plaintext. For full cryptographic details, see [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md).\n\n## Tech Stack\n\n| Component          | Technology                                     |\n| :----------------- | :--------------------------------------------- |\n| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS           |\n| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript                  |\n| **Database**       | PostgreSQL 16                                  |\n| **Job Queue**      | BullMQ + Redis                                 |\n| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth MPC Core Kit                          |\n| **Storage**        | IPFS via Kubo                                  |\n| **Desktop**        | Tauri v2 + FUSE-T (macOS) / WinFsp (Windows)  |\n| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud (IPNS republishing)                |\n| **Crypto**         | Web Crypto API (AES-256-GCM/CTR, ECIES secp256k1) |\n| **Observability**  | Grafana Faro (web), Prometheus (API)           |\n\n## Project Structure\n\n```text\ncipher-box/\n├── apps/\n│   ├── api/              # NestJS backend\n│   ├── web/              # React frontend\n│   ├── desktop/          # Tauri v2 desktop app\n│   └── tee-worker/       # Phala Cloud TEE worker\n├── packages/\n│   ├── core/             # Shared TypeScript types and metadata schemas\n│   ├── crypto/           # Shared encryption library (AES, ECIES, key derivation)\n│   ├── sdk-core/         # Stateless SDK operations (encrypt, pin, folder ops)\n│   ├── sdk/              # Stateful SDK client (CipherBoxClient)\n│   └── api-client/       # Generated OpenAPI typed client\n├── crates/\n│   ├── core/             # Shared Rust types and metadata schemas\n│   ├── crypto/           # Rust encryption library\n│   ├── sdk/              # Rust SDK (stateful orchestration, sync daemon)\n│   ├── fuse/             # FUSE filesystem implementation (macOS + Windows)\n│   └── api-client/       # Rust API client\n├── tests/\n│   ├── web-e2e/          # Playwright E2E tests (14 suites)\n│   ├── sdk-e2e/          # SDK integration tests (11 suites)\n│   ├── desktop-e2e/      # Desktop E2E tests\n│   ├── load/             # Load and performance tests\n│   └── vectors/          # Crypto test vectors\n└── docs/                 # Architecture, development, and specification docs\n```\n\n## Getting Started\n\n```bash\ndocker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d\npnpm install\npnpm dev   # starts API (:3000) + web (:5173)\n```\n\nSee [docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md) for full setup instructions including desktop app, testing, and environment configuration.\n\n## Documentation\n\n| Document                                                                         | Description                                     |\n| :------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- |\n| [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md)                                     | Encryption hierarchy, key derivation, threat model |\n| [docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md)                                       | Local setup, running, testing                   |\n| [docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md)       | Auth flow details                               |\n| [docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md)                             | All metadata object schemas                     |\n| [docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md](docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md)             | Filesystem rules, naming, and constraints       |\n| [docs/VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md](docs/VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md)                       | Export/recovery data format                     |\n| [docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md](docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md)        | Migration discipline                            |\n| [docs/CAPACITY.md](docs/CAPACITY.md)                                             | Storage quotas and limits                       |\n| [tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md](tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md)                           | SDK E2E and load testing architecture           |\n\n## License\n\n[MIT](LICENSE)\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md",
  "content": "# CipherBox Filesystem Specification\n\nThis document defines the rules, constraints, and rationale governing the CipherBox virtual filesystem. These rules apply across all platforms (web, desktop macOS, desktop Windows) and are enforced at different layers depending on the operation.\n\n## Design Principles\n\nCipherBox must present a consistent filesystem experience across three very different access surfaces:\n\n1. **Web browser** — drag-and-drop uploads, folder tree UI\n2. **macOS desktop** — FUSE-T virtual mount via SMB backend, accessed through Finder\n3. **Windows desktop** — WinFsp virtual mount, accessed through Explorer\n\nThe strictest platform constraint wins. Windows is case-insensitive and has the most restricted filename rules, so CipherBox defaults to rules compatible with Windows even when accessed from other platforms. This ensures files created on any platform are accessible on every other platform.\n\n## Naming Rules\n\n### Case Handling\n\n| Platform | Storage     | Lookup        | Implementation                    |\n| -------- | ----------- | ------------- | --------------------------------- |\n| Web      | As-entered  | Case-sensitive (`===`) | `folder.service.ts`, `sdk-core/folder` |\n| macOS    | As-entered  | NFC-normalized | `inode.rs:normalize_name()` via `unicode-normalization` crate |\n| Windows  | As-entered  | Case-insensitive (lowercase key) | `inode.rs:normalize_name()` via `.to_lowercase()` |\n\n**Rationale:** Original casing is always preserved in the encrypted metadata (`InodeData.name` / `FolderChild.name`). The normalization only affects HashMap key lookups in the FUSE layer. On macOS, NFC normalization prevents mismatches between composed and decomposed Unicode forms (e.g., `e` + combining acute vs. precomposed `e`). On Windows, lowercase folding implements the case-insensitive semantics that Explorer and all Windows applications expect.\n\n**Current gap:** The web UI uses strict case-sensitive matching for duplicate detection. A user could create `Report.pdf` and `report.pdf` in the same folder on the web, but these would collide when the vault is mounted on Windows. This is a known limitation — the web layer does not currently enforce Windows-compatible case-insensitive uniqueness.\n\n### Character Restrictions\n\n| Character              | Status    | Rationale                                            |\n| ---------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |\n| Null byte (`\\0`)       | Forbidden | Invalid in all filesystem APIs; rejected by UTF-8 validation in FUSE callbacks |\n| Full UTF-8 range       | Allowed   | Stored encrypted in metadata; no server-side interpretation |\n| Emoji and CJK          | Allowed   | Full Unicode support across all platforms            |\n| Path separators (`/`, `\\`) | Not validated | Names are stored in flat per-folder child arrays, not as paths |\n\n**Note:** Windows reserves additional characters (`<`, `>`, `:`, `\"`, `|`, `?`, `*`) in filenames. CipherBox does not currently validate against these at the web or SDK layer. Files created with these characters on the web would fail to materialize on a Windows FUSE mount. This is a known gap.\n\n### Reserved Names\n\nCipherBox filters platform-specific system files that should never be created, synced, or displayed. These are silently rejected during file creation (EACCES) and hidden from directory listings in the FUSE layer.\n\n**Cross-platform filter** (`helpers.rs:is_platform_special()`):\n\n| Pattern                      | Platform | Purpose                       |\n| ---------------------------- | -------- | ----------------------------- |\n| `._*` (prefix)               | macOS    | Resource fork files           |\n| `.DS_Store`                  | macOS    | Finder metadata               |\n| `.Trashes`                   | macOS    | Trash directory               |\n| `.fseventsd`                 | macOS    | File system events            |\n| `.Spotlight-V100`            | macOS    | Spotlight index               |\n| `.hidden`, `.localized`      | macOS    | Finder display hints          |\n| `.metadata_never_index*`     | macOS    | Spotlight indexing hints       |\n| `.ql_disablecache`           | macOS    | Quick Look cache control      |\n| `.ql_disablethumbnails`      | macOS    | Quick Look thumbnail control  |\n| `DCIM`                       | macOS    | Digital camera images dir     |\n| `Thumbs.db`                  | Windows  | Explorer thumbnail cache      |\n| `desktop.ini`                | Windows  | Explorer folder customization |\n| `.directory`                 | Linux    | KDE directory metadata        |\n| `.Trash-*` (prefix)          | Linux    | Per-user trash directories    |\n| `.gvfs`                      | Linux    | GNOME virtual filesystem      |\n| `.xdg-volume-info`           | Linux    | XDG volume information        |\n\n**Windows-specific filter** (`helpers.rs:is_windows_special()`):\n\n| Pattern                        | Purpose                              |\n| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------ |\n| `$recycle.bin`                 | Recycle bin directory                |\n| `System Volume Information`   | NTFS system metadata                 |\n| `Recycler`                    | Legacy recycle bin                    |\n| `pagefile.sys`, `swapfile.sys` | Virtual memory files                |\n| `hiberfil.sys`                | Hibernation file                     |\n| `$*` (prefix)                  | NTFS alternate data streams          |\n| `*:Zone.Identifier*`          | Downloaded file security zone marker |\n\n**Rationale:** These files are created automatically by operating systems and file managers. Syncing them wastes storage quota, creates noise in the file browser, and can cause errors when the vault is accessed from a different platform. Filtering at the FUSE layer (not the web layer) means the web UI has no awareness of these restrictions — files with these names can be uploaded via the web. They simply won't appear when the vault is mounted on desktop.\n\n**Not currently filtered:** Windows reserved device names (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`). These would cause issues on Windows FUSE mounts but are unlikely to be created intentionally.\n\n## Size Limits\n\n### File Size\n\n| Limit         | Value  | Enforcement                           | Constant                    |\n| ------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |\n| Max file size | 100 MB | Client-side (web upload), server-side (API) | `MAX_FILE_SIZE` in `useDropUpload.ts`, `MaxFileSizeValidator` in `ipfs.controller.ts` |\n\n**Rationale:** 100 MB balances usability with memory constraints. The entire file must fit in memory for AES-256-GCM encryption (authenticated encryption requires the full plaintext). AES-256-CTR streaming mode could support larger files but is currently only used for decryption/playback, not upload. The Web Worker encryption offload helps with CPU blocking but does not change the memory requirement.\n\n### Storage Quota\n\n| Limit       | Value   | Enforcement                          | Constant         |\n| ----------- | ------- | ------------------------------------ | ---------------- |\n| Total quota | 500 MiB | Server-side (`vault.service.ts`), client pre-check | `QUOTA_LIMIT_BYTES` / `QUOTA_BYTES` |\n\n**Rationale:** Technology demonstrator limit. Quota tracks encrypted blob sizes (not plaintext sizes). BYO-IPFS users bypass this limit (advisory only) since they manage their own storage.\n\n## Folder Structure\n\n### Depth Limit\n\n| Limit            | Value     | Enforcement                                  | Constant           |\n| ---------------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |\n| Max folder depth | 20 levels | Client-side (create folder, move folder)     | `MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH`  |\n\n**Rationale:** Each folder level requires a separate IPNS resolve + AES-256-GCM decrypt to traverse. Deep nesting creates latency during navigation and increases the number of IPNS records the TEE must republish. 20 levels is generous for practical use while bounding operational costs.\n\n**Enforcement points:**\n- `folder.service.ts:170` — `createFolder` checks `parentDepth >= MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH`\n- `folder.service.ts:759` — `moveFolder` checks `destDepth + 1 + subtreeDepth > MAX_FOLDER_DEPTH`\n- `useFolderMutations.ts:73` — duplicate check in React hooks layer\n\n### Duplicate Names\n\nFiles and folders within the same parent must have unique names. Duplicate detection is case-sensitive at the web/SDK layer.\n\n**Enforcement:** Before any create, rename, or move operation, the target folder's children are scanned:\n\n```typescript\nconst nameExists = children.some((c) => c.name === newName && c.id !== childId);\nif (nameExists) throw new Error('An item with this name already exists');\n```\n\n**Cross-type collision:** A file cannot have the same name as a folder in the same parent. The web upload layer explicitly checks for file-to-folder name collisions before starting uploads.\n\n**Batch upload deduplication:** Within a single batch upload, duplicate filenames are rejected before any files are read into memory.\n\n## File Versioning\n\n| Limit               | Value       | Enforcement                  | Constant                 |\n| -------------------- | ----------- | ---------------------------- | ------------------------ |\n| Max versions/file    | 10          | SDK and FUSE layer           | `MAX_VERSIONS_PER_FILE`  |\n| Version cooldown     | 15 minutes  | Desktop FUSE only            | `VERSION_COOLDOWN_MS`    |\n\n**Rationale:** Version history is stored inside the file's IPNS metadata. Each version entry contains a CID, key, IV, and timestamp. Capping at 10 prevents metadata bloat. The 15-minute cooldown on desktop prevents rapid saves (e.g., auto-save in text editors) from consuming all version slots.\n\n**Web behavior:** The web UI creates a new version on every file replace operation. There is no cooldown — the user explicitly triggers each replace via the upload dialog.\n\n## FUSE Mount Specifics\n\n### Content Download\n\n| Parameter          | Value    | Constant                    |\n| ------------------ | -------- | --------------------------- |\n| Download timeout   | 120s     | `CONTENT_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT`  |\n| Block size         | 4096 B   | `BLOCK_SIZE`                |\n\n**Rationale:** File content is fetched from IPFS on `open()` and cached in memory. Large files (up to 100 MB) can take 30-60 seconds from the staging IPFS gateway. The 120-second timeout accommodates slow networks while preventing indefinite hangs. After the initial fetch, all `read()` calls are served from cache.\n\n### Mount Backend\n\n| Platform | Backend | Notes                                                           |\n| -------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| macOS    | SMB     | FUSE-T's SMB backend. NFS backend has unfixable kernel bug (WRITE RPCs never reach FUSE-T for new files). |\n| Windows  | WinFsp  | Native user-mode filesystem driver.                             |\n| Linux    | libfuse | Standard FUSE. Implemented but less tested than macOS/Windows.  |\n\n### Inode Management\n\n- **Root inode:** Always `1` (standard FUSE convention)\n- **Inode stability:** `populate_folder` reuses existing inode numbers for same-name children. Allocating new inodes for unchanged files causes NFS \"stale file handle\" disconnects on macOS.\n- **Lazy loading:** Folder children are populated on first `readdir`/`lookup`, not on mount. This avoids loading the entire vault tree upfront.\n\n## Encryption Modes\n\n| Mode        | Usage                              | File size threshold | Notes                                          |\n| ----------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |\n| AES-256-GCM | Default for all files              | Any                 | Authenticated encryption; full file in memory   |\n| AES-256-CTR | Large media streaming (decryption) | > 256 KB (video)    | Enables streaming playback without full decrypt |\n\n**Rationale:** GCM provides authentication (tamper detection) but requires the entire plaintext in memory. CTR mode is used only for decryption/playback of large media files where streaming is essential for UX. Uploads always use GCM. The mode is recorded in file metadata so the correct decryption path is selected on download.\n\n## Metadata Storage\n\nFile and folder names, timestamps, and structural information are stored as encrypted JSON in IPNS records. The server never sees plaintext names or folder structure. See [METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) for the full schema reference and [METADATA_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md](METADATA_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md) for change management rules.\n\n## Known Gaps\n\nThese are documented limitations where cross-platform consistency is not yet enforced:\n\n1. **Web case-sensitivity vs. Windows case-insensitivity** — Files created via the web with names differing only by case will collide on Windows desktop mount.\n2. **Windows reserved characters** — Characters like `<`, `>`, `:`, `\"`, `|`, `?`, `*` are not validated at the web/SDK layer. Files with these characters will fail on Windows FUSE mounts.\n3. **Windows reserved device names** — `CON`, `PRN`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9` are not validated.\n4. **Path length** — No total path length validation. Windows has a 260-character path limit (unless long path support is enabled). Deep nesting + long names could exceed this on Windows.\n5. **Leading/trailing spaces and dots** — Windows silently strips these from filenames. Files with trailing dots/spaces created on the web would appear with different names on Windows.\n\n<!-- Filesystem specification: 2026-03-30 -->\n"
}
```

id: toolu_01K5aB3LhnX2fvxCq7Yev2Qa
```
1	---
2	created: 2026-02-07T12:00
3	title: Offload large file encryption to Web Worker
4	area: ui
5	files:
6	  - apps/web/src/services/file-crypto.service.ts
7	  - apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts
8	---
9	
10	## Problem
11	
12	`encryptFile()` in `file-crypto.service.ts` calls `file.arrayBuffer()` and `encryptAesGcm()` on the main thread. For large files (approaching the 100MB limit), this blocks the UI thread and can cause the browser to appear frozen during encryption.
13	
14	Identified by CodeRabbit review on PR #55.
15	
16	## Solution
17	
18	Offload encryption to a Web Worker for files >= 10MB:
19	
20	1. Create a dedicated encryption worker (`encrypt.worker.ts`)
21	2. Worker receives `File`/`Blob`, generates file key + IV, runs `encryptAesGcm`, returns ciphertext + wrapped key
22	3. Main thread stays responsive, upload progress UI remains smooth
23	4. Files < 10MB continue using main thread (worker overhead not worth it for small files)
24	
25	**Considerations:**
26	
27	- Web Crypto API is available in Worker contexts
28	- Need to handle `Transferable` objects for zero-copy ArrayBuffer passing
29	- Worker bundling with Vite uses `new Worker(new URL(...), { type: 'module' })` pattern
30	- Error handling: worker crashes should fall back to main thread encryption
31	
```

> DEVELOPER

The mention of lack of Desktop FUSE automated tests is also misleading - there aren't unit tests as these would require mocking too much of the host OS to be reliable. Those are intentionally not tested at a unit level, since it is mostly jsut plumbing between the OS and the rust SDK. These components are recularly tested in the `Desktop E2E` test suite, so saying there is no automated testing is false, its just not unit testing.

The source for this in @.planning/codebase/CONCERNS.md should also be updated as irrelevant (wont fix) with the details of the above note.

  "new_string": "**FUSE write operations have no unit tests (won't fix):**\n\n- What's not tested: Write operation implementations (create file, write data, rename, delete, mkdir, publish coordination) in both macOS and Windows variants.\n- Files: `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` (976 lines), `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs` (1008 lines)\n- Status: Won't fix. That is the appropriate test level for OS integration code."

## Security Review Findings Verification Report

I've verified the current state of all four security findings against the codebase. Here's what I found:

### **1. M1: itemName stored plaintext on server** - CONFIRMED OPEN

**Status:** Open (still plaintext, not encrypted)

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/entities/share.entity.ts` (lines 45-50)
  ```
  @Column({ type: 'varchar', length: 255, name: 'item_name' })
  itemName!: string;
  ```
  
- The column comment explicitly documents this is plaintext with a privacy justification:
  ```
  /**
   * Display name of the shared item (plaintext).
   * Privacy impact is minimal -- server already knows user IDs involved.
   */
  ```

- **Confirmation in create flow:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts` (line 96)
  - itemName is stored directly from the DTO without encryption

**Finding:** itemName remains plaintext. Not encrypted with recipient's public key as the security review recommended.

---

### **2. M5: reWrapForRecipients silently swallows errors** - PARTIALLY MITIGATED (but still swallows)

**Status:** Open (errors are caught silently, but now emits an event)

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/share/index.ts` (lines 80-126)
  ```typescript
  export async function reWrapForRecipients(params: {
    coveringShares: SentShareInfo[];
    newItems: Array<{
      keyType: 'file' | 'folder';
      itemId: string;
      plaintextKey: Uint8Array;
    }>;
    addShareKeysFn: (
      shareId: string,
      keys: Array<{ keyType: 'file' | 'folder'; itemId: string; encryptedKey: string }>
    ) => Promise<void>;
  }): Promise<{ failedRecipients: string[] }> {
    ...
    for (const share of params.coveringShares) {
      try {
        ...
        await params.addShareKeysFn(share.shareId, wrappedKeys);
      } catch {
        failedRecipients.push(share.recipientPublicKey);
      }
    }
    return { failedRecipients };
  }
  ```

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (lines 132-147)
  - The catch block swallows errors completely without details
  - Does emit a `share:reWrapFailed` event (line 144), but individual error details are lost
  - No background retry mechanism

**Finding:** Errors are caught silently at line 120 with bare `catch {}`. While the client emits an event, the error details are not captured or logged, and there's no retry mechanism.

---

### **3. L1: /shares/lookup enables public key enumeration** - CONFIRMED VULNERABLE

**Status:** Open (still returns different HTTP responses)

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.controller.ts` (lines 150-172)
  ```typescript
  @Get('lookup')
  @ApiOperation({
    summary: 'Look up user by public key',
    description: 'Verify a public key belongs to a registered CipherBox user.',
  })
  @ApiQuery({
    name: 'publicKey',
    description: 'Uncompressed secp256k1 public key (0x04...)',
    required: true,
  })
  @ApiResponse({ status: 200, description: 'Lookup result', type: LookupUserResponseDto })
  @ApiResponse({ status: 400, description: 'Invalid public key format' })
  @ApiResponse({ status: 401, description: 'Unauthorized' })
  async lookupUser(@Query('publicKey') publicKey: string): Promise<LookupUserResponseDto> {
    if (!publicKey || !/^0x04[0-9a-fA-F]{128}$/.test(publicKey)) {
      throw new BadRequestException(...);
    }

    const exists = await this.sharesService.lookupUserByPublicKey(publicKey);
    return { exists };
  }
  ```

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/dto/lookup-user-response.dto.ts` (lines 3-6)
  ```typescript
  export class LookupUserResponseDto {
    @ApiProperty({ description: 'Whether a registered user was found for the given public key' })
    exists!: boolean;
  }
  ```

**Finding:** The endpoint returns `{ exists: boolean }` but still uses HTTP 200 always. However, the architecture suggests authenticated enumeration is still possible - an attacker with authentication can call this repeatedly to enumerate registered keys. The endpoint does require `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)` (line 40 of controller) but this only stops unauthenticated enumeration, not authenticated enumeration attacks.

---

### **4. L4: No pagination on shares endpoints** - IMPLEMENTED

**Status:** Implemented - Pagination is now active

**Evidence:**
- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/dto/pagination.dto.ts` (lines 1-50)
  - Defines `PaginationQueryDto` with `limit` (default 50, max 100) and `offset` (default 0)

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.controller.ts`
  - **Line 93:** `@Query() pagination: PaginationQueryDto` on `getReceivedShares()`
  - **Line 129:** `@Query() pagination: PaginationQueryDto` on `getSentShares()`

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts`
  - **Lines 135-152:** `getReceivedShares()` uses `take: limit, skip: offset`
  - **Lines 158-174:** `getSentShares()` uses `take: limit, skip: offset`

**Finding:** Pagination is fully implemented with sensible defaults and validation.

---

## Deferred Items Verification Report

Based on a thorough codebase review, here's the status of each deferred sharing/collaboration item:

### 1. **Metadata-embedded sharing** - STILL DEFERRED
Shares are **stored in the database**, not on IPFS metadata. Evidence:
- `/apps/api/src/shares/entities/share.entity.ts` defines a database-backed Share entity with database columns for `sharerId`, `recipientId`, `encryptedKey`, `permission`, etc.
- `/packages/core/src/folder/types.ts` and `/packages/core/src/file/types.ts` contain only content metadata (file/folder structure), not sharing metadata
- The share entity stores the encrypted key wrapped for recipients, but the share record itself lives in PostgreSQL

### 2. **Attribution/audit trail (lastModifiedBy)** - STILL DEFERRED
No `lastModifiedBy` or attribution fields found. Evidence:
- `/packages/core/src/folder/types.ts` contains only `modifiedAt` (timestamp), no `lastModifiedBy` field
- `/packages/core/src/file/types.ts` contains only `modifiedAt` (timestamp), no `lastModifiedBy` field
- No grep results for `lastModifiedBy`, `modifiedBy`, or attribution fields anywhere in the codebase

### 3. **Transitive re-sharing** - STILL DEFERRED
Share recipients **cannot reshare**. Evidence:
- `/packages/sdk/src/share/shared-write.ts` implements write operations for recipients (upload, rename, delete) but has NO resharing functionality
- Share recipients have `recipientPublicKey` but the system does not allow them to create new shares on received items
- No API endpoint or SDK method exists for recipients to share received items with other users

### 4. **Share notifications** - STILL DEFERRED
There is **no notification system for permission changes**. Evidence:
- `/apps/web/src/stores/notification.store.ts` exists but is a generic notification store for info/warning/error messages
- Zero grep results for "share notification", "permission notification", or "revoke notification"
- No webhook or push notification system for share events
- Notifications are manual/UI-only, triggered by user actions, not broadcasted

### 5. **User discovery service** - PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED
User lookup by public key exists, but **no email/username discovery**. Evidence:
- `/apps/api/src/shares/shares.controller.ts` has `lookupUser()` endpoint (lines 150-172) that accepts a public key and returns `{ exists: boolean }`
- `/apps/api/src/shares/dto/lookup-user-response.dto.ts` only returns existence status, no user profile/display name
- Public key is the only lookup mechanism; no email or username-based discovery

### 6. **Display names for share recipients** - STILL DEFERRED
Share recipients shown **only by public key**, no display names. Evidence:
- `/apps/api/src/shares/dto/share-response.dto.ts` shows `recipientPublicKey` and `sharerPublicKey` fields only
- No name, email, or display name fields in share DTOs
- Web UI references `share.recipientPublicKey` with truncation but no name mapping

### 7. **Immediate key rotation on revoke** - LAZY ROTATION (Deferred Immediate)
Key rotation is **lazy, not immediate**. Evidence:
- `/apps/api/src/shares/entities/share.entity.ts` has comment: "Soft-delete timestamp for lazy key rotation. null = active share, timestamp = revoked pending rotation. On next folder modification, sharer rotates folderKey and hard-deletes."
- `/packages/sdk/src/share/index.ts` `revokeShare()` is a thin wrapper that just calls the revoke API
- `/apps/web/src/services/share.service.ts` implements `executeLazyRotation()` which executes rotation **only when the folder is next modified**, not immediately
- The `revokedAt` field is set on revoke, but actual key rotation is deferred

### 8. **CRDT-based IPNS inbox** - NOT IMPLEMENTED
No CRDT implementation found. Evidence:
- Zero grep results for "CRDT", "crdt", "Yjs", or "automerge" in the entire codebase
- IPNS records are deterministic (single source of truth per folder IPNS name)

### 9. **Faster sync for shared folders (10s poll)** - STILL USING 30s INTERVAL
Polling is **30 seconds, not 10 seconds**. Evidence:
- `/apps/web/src/hooks/useSyncPolling.ts` line 8: `const SYNC_INTERVAL_MS = 30000;` (30 seconds)
- `/apps/web/src/hooks/useSharedNavigation.ts` line 132: "Polling interval ref for 30s sync"
- Comment in useSyncPolling.ts: "Polls every 30s when tab is visible and online" per CONTEXT.md

| Accumulated retry batching                      | 37           | Batch retries into single folder publish instead of N individual publishes                |
| Batch upload secondary pin warning events       | 37           | BYO-IPFS `pinFn` wrapper discards `secondaryWarning`; `pin:secondaryFailed` never emitted |
| AbortSignal support for in-flight batch uploads | 37           | No way to cancel once `uploadFiles()` invoked; needs AbortSignal through p-limit          |
| Lazy file reading within concurrency pool       | 37           | `useDropUpload` reads all files upfront; SDK needs `File` objects or read callback        |
