1	# CipherBox Memory
2	
3	## CRITICAL: Branch Protection & Signing Trust
4	- **NEVER commit directly to `main`** — no exceptions, not even docs/todo moves
5	- Commit signing requirement **disabled** on branch protection (2026-02-13) — GSD agent subprocesses can't access 1Password SSH agent
6	- Tag protection still requires signatures; GitHub signs merge/release commits automatically
7	- Git config `commit.gpgsign=true` still set — signing happens when 1Password agent is available
8	- **Do NOT intentionally bypass signing** — no `-c commit.gpgsign=false`, no `--no-gpg-sign`
9	- Unsigned commits from GSD agents are acceptable since PRs are squash-merged by GitHub with its own signature
10	- **First action when starting ANY task:** determine the feature branch name, then switch to it
11	  - If on `main`: `git checkout -b feat/<name>` immediately, before ANY other work
12	  - If on a feature branch: verify it's the RIGHT branch for this task
13	  - If on the WRONG feature branch: `git checkout main && git pull && git checkout -b feat/<new-name>`
14	- Always branch off from latest `main` (`git pull` first)
15	- **Before pushing follow-up commits to a branch**, check if the PR was already merged: `gh pr list --head <branch> --state merged`
16	  - CI workflow fixes often get merged quickly, so always verify before pushing additional commits
17	  - If merged: `git checkout main && git pull && git checkout -b fix/<new-name>` and cherry-pick or redo the change
18	- The todo-move step in /gsd:check-todos must also happen on the feature branch, not main
19	- This rule applies to ALL commit types: code, docs, config, todo moves, state updates — everything
20	
21	## Project Status
22	- [project_milestone_v11.md](project_milestone_v11.md) — v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure milestone complete (2026-03-30), 22 phases, 86 plans
23	
24	## SDK Testing & Load Testing Strategy
25	- [project_testing_strategy.md](project_testing_strategy.md) — SDK E2E / load test architecture, rate limiting bypass, setApiClientConfig caveat
26	- In-repo doc: `tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`
27	
28	## Dev Environment
29	- Docker is NOT needed to start services - all Docker dependencies (PostgreSQL, IPFS, Redis) run on the host machine at `192.168.133.12`
30	- API `.env` points DB/IPFS/Redis to `192.168.133.12` (ports: PG 5432, IPFS 5001/8080, Redis 6380)
31	- Just start the API (`pnpm --filter api dev`) and frontend (`pnpm --filter web dev`)
32	- Frontend: http://localhost:5173
33	- API: http://localhost:3000
34	- For SDK E2E tests: set `NODE_ENV=test` in `apps/api/.env` (relaxes throttle limits), start mock-ipns-routing, then run tests
35	
36	## Playwright Login
37	- E2E credentials in `tests/web-e2e/.env`: email `test_account_4718@example.com`, OTP `851527`
38	- Flow: Click [CONNECT] -> Continue with Email/Phone -> enter email -> submit -> enter OTP digits -> wait ~10s for auth
39	
40	## Parallel Agent Work
41	- **Use git worktrees for parallel agents**, not branch switching on a shared working tree
42	- Always commit lockfile changes when adding dev dependencies (CI uses `--frozen-lockfile`)
43	- [feedback_worktrees.md](feedback_worktrees.md) — NEVER remove/reuse existing worktrees; always create new ones with unique names
44	
45	## FUSE-T Key Lessons
46	- **SWITCHED TO SMB BACKEND**: NFS backend has unfixable macOS kernel bug — WRITE RPCs never reach FUSE-T for new files. SMB backend works correctly. Mount option: `backend=smb`.
47	- **Vendored fuser with socket-read patch**: FUSE-T communicates via Unix domain socket, not /dev/fuse. Stock fuser does single read() expecting atomic messages, but sockets fragment large messages and coalesce small ones. Fix: peek at 4-byte header length, then loop-read exactly that many bytes. Patch in `vendor/fuser/src/channel.rs`.
48	- **SMB opendir must return non-zero fh**: SMB backend treats `fh=0` as invalid handle. Use `next_fh.fetch_add(1)`.
49	- **SMB rename FIXED**: Was failing with EPERM because `access(W_OK)` checked `req.uid() != attr.uid` — under SMB, UIDs differ. Fix: `access()` always grants (encryption is access control). UID in `create()`/`mkdir()` unified to `getuid()`.
50	- **FUSE-T truncates filenames in rename callback** by 8 bytes — need suffix-match fallback
51	- **NFS single-thread**: ALL callbacks on one thread. ANY blocking call stalls everything.
52	- **NEVER block in read()**: Even 500ms blocks cause Finder "connection lost". Use channel-based prefetch.
53	- **readdir/lookup consistency is critical**: NFS client does LOOKUP for every readdir entry including ".." — ENOENT causes disconnect
54	- **NFS inode stability is critical**: populate_folder MUST reuse existing inode numbers. Allocating new inos for same-name children causes NFS "stale file handle" disconnects.
55	- **Background refresh race**: async metadata refresh can overwrite local mutations. Use mutation cooldown (30s).
56	- **Deduplicate readdir refreshes**: NFS calls readdir at offset=0 and offset=N — only fire refresh on offset=0.
57	- **macOS keychain**: `keyring::set_password()` fails if item exists — always delete first. Debug builds skip Keychain entirely (each rebuild = new binary signature = repeated prompts).
58	- **Stale mount cleanup**: After crash, ~/CipherBox may contain .DS_Store. App should rm contents before mount.
59	- **Force unmount**: `umount` fails when Finder has handles open. Use `diskutil unmount force` as fallback.
60	- **FUSE-T logs**: Check `~/Library/Logs/fuse-t/fuse-t.log` for NFS/SMB-level errors.
61	
62	## Tauri Webview Lessons
63	- **Web3Auth clearCache() vs logout({cleanup:true})**: cleanup:true tears down connectors, causing "Wallet connector not ready". Use clearCache() to clear session without destroying SDK state.
64	- **Window reuse**: Existing window created with on_new_window handler keeps it across show/hide. Don't destroy+recreate (label conflict). Just reload page with eval("location.reload()").
65	- **OAuth popup cleanup**: Destroy oauth-popup-* windows after auth completes in Rust handle_auth_complete.
66	
67	## Staging Environment
68	- **VPS:** 76.13.151.200 (Hostinger), SSH key `~/.ssh/ci-deploy-key`
69	- **API:** https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc (Caddy reverse proxy)
70	- **Web:** https://app-staging.cipherbox.cc (Caddy static files)
71	- **Domains:** use flat subdomains (`api-staging`, not `api.staging`) — Cloudflare Universal SSL only covers `*.cipherbox.cc`
72	- **Deploy:** push tag `staging-v<version>-rc-<N>` (e.g., `staging-v0.26.0-rc-1`) triggers `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`
73	- **GitHub Environment:** `staging` — secrets/vars split (vars for non-sensitive, secrets for sensitive)
74	- **Docker Compose `.env`:** must be named `.env` (not `.env.staging`) for variable substitution
75	- **pnpm Docker:** use `pnpm deploy --prod --legacy` for flat node_modules without symlinks
76	- See `.learnings/2026-02-09-staging-deployment-first-deploy.md` for full details
77	
78	## Pencil MCP Lessons
79	- **Changes are in-memory only** — `batch_design` does NOT write to `.pen` file on disk. User must save from Pencil editor.
80	- **Copy gives new IDs**: After `C()`, must `batch_get` to discover new descendant IDs. Never `U()` descendants of a just-copied node in the same batch.
81	- See `.learnings/2026-02-09-pencil-mcp-multi-screen-design.md` for full details.
82	
83	## CRITICAL: TypeORM Migration Discipline
84	- **`synchronize: true` in dev/test hides missing migrations** — tables auto-created from entities
85	- **Every new `@Entity()` MUST have a corresponding `CREATE TABLE` migration**
86	- **Use `IF NOT EXISTS`** for idempotency
87	- **Migration timestamp ordering matters** — create-table must precede any table modification
88	- **Full protocol:** `docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md`
89	
90	## CRITICAL: Run E2E Tests Before Pushing
91	- **Always run the relevant E2E test suite before pushing** — CI feedback takes 15-20 min, local/staging runs are immediate
92	- **Run against staging** (most reliable): `BASE_URL=https://app-staging.cipherbox.cc pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web-e2e exec playwright test tests/<feature>.spec.ts --timeout 180000`
93	- **NEVER commit E2E fixes blind and hope CI passes** — always repro locally first
94	- See `.learnings/2026-03-29-e2e-test-debugging-workflow.md`
95	
96	## Conventional Commits & Branch Naming
97	- `fix:` implies a **bug fix** and triggers a **patch version bump** — do not use for config cleanup
98	- `chore:` is correct for non-functional changes (config, tooling, dependency cleanup)
99	- `feat:` triggers a **minor version bump** — only use for actual new features
100	- Branch prefix should match the commit type
101	- When in doubt, prefer `chore:` over `fix:`
102	
103	## Patterns & Lessons
104	- Always verify state machines end-to-end in UAT, not just individual transitions
105	- For async deduplication, share the Promise, not a boolean flag
106	- Never use eventually-consistent external services as sole source of truth
107	- Playwright UAT catches UX issues that code review and static verification miss
108	- **Zustand stale closures in async callbacks**: Use `useFolderStore.getState()` inside async callbacks, not hook selectors
109	- **GitHub PR review comments workflow**: See `.learnings/2026-02-10-pr-review-comment-workflow.md`
110	- **Don't justify code duplication with build optimization**: maintainability matters more than dead-code elimination
111	- **GSD UI keyword detection** must be kept in sync across 4 locations: `quick.md`, `discuss-phase.md`, `design/sync.md`, `execute-phase.md`
112	
113	## GSD Workflow Branching
114	- [feedback_gsd_branching.md](feedback_gsd_branching.md) — All GSD steps for a phase stay on ONE branch; never create new branches mid-workflow
115	
116	## API Console OTP
117	- [feedback_api_console_otp.md](feedback_api_console_otp.md) — grep task output files for OTPs, don't overcomplicate
118	
119	## Fix Test Type Errors Proactively
120	- [feedback_fix_test_errors.md](feedback_fix_test_errors.md) — Always fix test type errors when noticed during typecheck
121	
122	## E2E Test Debugging
123	- [feedback_e2e_debugging.md](feedback_e2e_debugging.md) — NEVER push E2E fixes blind; always repro against staging first
124	- [feedback_worktree_env.md](feedback_worktree_env.md) — Fresh worktrees need .env files + pnpm install; web-only changes use staging API
125	
126	## Metadata Schema Documentation
127	- **Schema reference:** `docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md` — all 10 metadata objects with field tables
128	- **Evolution protocol:** `docs/METADATA_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md` — formal rules for schema changes
129	- **ALWAYS consult these docs** before any ticket that adds/removes/changes metadata fields
130	
```

1	import {
2	  initializeFaro,
3	  getWebInstrumentations,
4	  type Faro,
5	  type TransportItem,
6	  LogLevel,
7	} from '@grafana/faro-web-sdk';
8	import { TracingInstrumentation } from '@grafana/faro-web-tracing';
9	import { ReactIntegration } from '@grafana/faro-react';
10	
11	/** Field names containing crypto material or PII — scrubbed from all Faro payloads. */
12	const SENSITIVE_KEYS = new Set([
13	  'privateKey',
14	  'rootFolderKey',
15	  'folderKey',
16	  'fileKey',
17	  'accessToken',
18	  'ipnsPrivateKey',
19	  'teePublicKey',
20	  'userEmail',
21	  'email',
22	]);
23	
24	/** 64+ hex chars = 32+ byte key material */
25	const HEX_KEY_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-fA-F]{64,}$/;
26	
27	/** Matches hex key material embedded in URLs (invite links, hash fragments) */
28	const URL_HEX_KEY_PATTERN = /[0-9a-fA-F]{64,}/g;
29	
30	function scrubValue(value: unknown): unknown {
31	  if (typeof value === 'string') {
32	    if (value.length >= 64 && HEX_KEY_PATTERN.test(value)) {
33	      return '[REDACTED_KEY]';
34	    }
35	    // Scrub hex keys embedded in URLs (e.g., invite links with key material in hash/query)
36	    if (value.length > 64) {
37	      URL_HEX_KEY_PATTERN.lastIndex = 0;
38	      if (URL_HEX_KEY_PATTERN.test(value)) {
39	        URL_HEX_KEY_PATTERN.lastIndex = 0;
40	        return value.replace(URL_HEX_KEY_PATTERN, '[REDACTED_KEY]');
41	      }
42	    }
43	  }
44	  if (value instanceof ArrayBuffer || ArrayBuffer.isView(value)) {
45	    return '[REDACTED_BINARY]';
46	  }
47	  if (typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && 'buffer' in value && 'byteLength' in value) {
48	    return '[REDACTED_BINARY]';
49	  }
50	  return value;
51	}
52	
53	function scrubArray(arr: unknown[], depth: number): unknown[] {
54	  if (depth > 3) return ['[DEPTH_LIMIT]'];
55	  return arr.map((item) => {
56	    const s = scrubValue(item);
57	    if (s !== item) return s;
58	    if (Array.isArray(item)) return scrubArray(item, depth + 1);
59	    if (typeof item === 'object' && item !== null) {
60	      return scrubObject(item as Record<string, unknown>, depth + 1);
61	    }
62	    return item;
63	  });
64	}
65	
66	/** Recursively scrub sensitive keys/values. Beyond max depth, redacts rather than leaks. */
67	function scrubObject(obj: Record<string, unknown>, depth = 0): Record<string, unknown> {
68	  if (depth > 3) return { _redacted: '[DEPTH_LIMIT]' };
69	
70	  const result: Record<string, unknown> = {};
71	  for (const key of Object.keys(obj)) {
72	    const value = obj[key];
73	
74	    if (SENSITIVE_KEYS.has(key)) {
75	      result[key] = '[REDACTED]';
76	      continue;
77	    }
78	
79	    const scrubbed = scrubValue(value);
80	    if (scrubbed !== value) {
81	      result[key] = scrubbed;
82	      continue;
83	    }
84	
85	    if (Array.isArray(value)) {
86	      result[key] = scrubArray(value, depth + 1);
87	    } else if (typeof value === 'object' && value !== null) {
88	      result[key] = scrubObject(value as Record<string, unknown>, depth + 1);
89	    } else {
90	      result[key] = value;
91	    }
92	  }
93	  return result;
94	}
95	
96	/** Privacy gate: scrubs sensitive data before telemetry leaves the browser. */
97	function beforeSend(item: TransportItem): TransportItem | null {
98	  let modified = false;
99	  let payload = item.payload;
100	  let meta = item.meta;
101	
102	  if (payload && typeof payload === 'object') {
103	    payload = scrubObject(
104	      payload as unknown as Record<string, unknown>
105	    ) as TransportItem['payload'];
106	    modified = true;
107	  }
108	
109	  // Scrub meta (may contain nested sensitive values beyond just email)
110	  if (meta && typeof meta === 'object') {
111	    meta = scrubObject(meta as unknown as Record<string, unknown>) as TransportItem['meta'];
112	    modified = true;
113	  }
114	
115	  return modified ? { ...item, payload, meta } : item;
116	}

118	let faroInstance: Faro | undefined;

120	export function getFaroInstance(): Faro | undefined {
121	  return faroInstance;
122	}
123	
124	/** Initialize Grafana Faro. No-op when VITE_FARO_URL is absent (local dev). */
125	export function initFaro(): Faro | undefined {
126	  const faroUrl = import.meta.env.VITE_FARO_URL;
127	  if (!faroUrl) return undefined;
128	
129	  faroInstance = initializeFaro({
130	    url: faroUrl,
131	    app: {
132	      name: 'cipherbox-web',
133	      version: import.meta.env.VITE_APP_VERSION ?? 'dev',
134	      environment: import.meta.env.VITE_ENVIRONMENT ?? 'development',
135	    },
136	    instrumentations: [
137	      ...getWebInstrumentations({
138	        captureConsole: false, // Phase 28 logger handles console capture
139	      }),
140	      new TracingInstrumentation({
141	        instrumentationOptions: {
142	          // Prevent request/response bodies from reaching telemetry — may contain encrypted payloads
143	          fetchInstrumentationOptions: { ignoreNetworkEvents: true },
144	          xhrInstrumentationOptions: { ignoreNetworkEvents: true },
145	        },
146	      }),
147	      new ReactIntegration(),
148	    ],
149	    beforeSend,
150	    sessionTracking: {
151	      enabled: true,
152	      persistent: false, // Don't persist session across tabs
153	    },
154	  });
155	
156	  return faroInstance;
157	}

159	/** Set current user identity on Faro (publicKey only, never email). */
160	export function setFaroUser(publicKey: string): void {
161	  const faro = getFaroInstance();
162	  if (!faro) return;
163	  faro.api.setUser({ id: publicKey });
164	}
165	
166	/** Clear user identity from Faro (call on logout). */
167	export function clearFaroUser(): void {
168	  const faro = getFaroInstance();
169	  if (!faro) return;
170	  faro.api.resetUser();
171	}
172	
173	/**
174	 * Register a Faro transport that forwards warn/error log calls to Grafana.
175	 * Integrates with Phase 28's logger transports array. No-op if Faro is not initialized.
176	 */
177	export function registerFaroTransport(
178	  loggerTransports: Array<
179	    (level: string, message: string, context?: Record<string, unknown>) => void
180	  >
181	): void {
182	  const faro = getFaroInstance();
183	  if (!faro) return;
184	
185	  const toStringContext = (ctx: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, string> => {
186	    // Scrub sensitive values before forwarding to Faro
187	    const scrubbed = scrubObject(ctx);
188	    return Object.fromEntries(
189	      Object.entries(scrubbed)
190	        .filter(([k]) => k !== 'error')
191	        .map(([k, v]) => [k, typeof v === 'string' ? v : String(v)])
192	    );
193	  };
194	
195	  const faroTransport = (
196	    level: string,
197	    message: string,
198	    context?: Record<string, unknown>
199	  ): void => {
200	    if (level === 'error') {
201	      const error = context?.error instanceof Error ? context.error : new Error(message);
202	      faro.api.pushError(error, {
203	        context: context ? toStringContext(context) : undefined,
204	      });
205	    } else if (level === 'warn') {
206	      faro.api.pushLog([message], {
207	        level: LogLevel.WARN,
208	        context: context ? toStringContext(context) : undefined,
209	      });
210	    }
211	  };
212	
213	  loggerTransports.push(faroTransport);
214	}
215

## Summary of Code Quality Deferred Items Verification

### 1. **DTS circular build dependency (crypto ↔ core)** - Phase 19.1

**Current State: STILL VALID - Circular dependency exists and is being worked around**

Evidence:
- **packages/crypto/package.json**: Line 33 shows `@cipherbox/core` in devDependencies (workspace:*)
- **packages/core/package.json**: Line 24 shows `@cipherbox/crypto` in dependencies (workspace:*)
- **Rust crates**: crates/core/Cargo.toml depends on cipherbox-crypto (line 8), while crates/crypto/Cargo.toml has NO dependency on cipherbox-core
- **JavaScript circular pattern**: @cipherbox/crypto imports from @cipherbox/core **only in devDependencies** (for testing), while @cipherbox/core imports @cipherbox/crypto in production dependencies
- **TypeScript config**: No special path workarounds in tsconfig files - pure monorepo workspace resolution via pnpm
- **Why it works**: The crypto package's devDependency on core is development-only (testing), not runtime. The build succeeds because:
  1. Crypto builds first (no @cipherbox/core needed at runtime)
  2. Core builds after, importing crypto
  3. No runtime circular require occurs
- **Deferred status in DEFERRED.md**: Line 121 confirms this is still deferred with note "Workaround in place; not fixed"

**Recommendation: RESOLVED** - The circular dependency is inherent to the architecture (core needs crypto, crypto needs core for testing) and the current approach is sound. Document it as "by design" rather than fix it.

### 4. **useFileOperations.ts decomposition** - Phase 31

**Current State: NOT DECOMPOSED - File size is legitimate**

Evidence:
- **File status**: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useFileOperations.ts` exists with **exactly 515 lines**
- **Structure analysis**: 
  - React hook composition with 3 main callback functions:
    - `handleAddFile` (lines 44-170): Single file upload, ~130 lines
    - `handleAddFiles` (lines 182-324): Batch file upload, ~140 lines
    - `handleUpdateFile` (lines 339-516): File re-encryption/version, ~180 lines
  - All callbacks share state management (`useState` for loading/error)
  - Common imports for stores, services, and utilities (~22 lines)
- **Cohesion**: All three operations are closely related (file lifecycle in folder tree)
- **Usages** (found in 4 files):
  1. `apps/web/src/hooks/useFolder.ts` - composes useFileOperations
  2. `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/ReplaceFileDialog.tsx`
  3. `apps/web/src/services/share.service.ts` (indirect reference)
- **Deferred status in DEFERRED.md**: Line 124 notes "Not in the 900+ line targets; separate effort"
- **Size assessment**: 515 lines is reasonable for a focused React hook handling 3 related file operations with unified state management

**Recommendation: RESOLVED/IRRELEVANT** - The file size is appropriate for its scope. It could be split into separate hooks (useAddFile, useUpdateFile) but cohesion is high, making decomposition provide minimal benefit. Not a priority for retirement.

470	### Phase 27: Writable Shares (PoC)
471	
472	**Goal:** Extend Phase 14's read-only sharing to support read-write shares, leveraging existing server-side optimistic concurrency (expectedSequenceNumber / 409 conflict detection) to coordinate multi-writer IPNS publishes.
473	**Requirements**: SHARE-01, SHARE-02, SHARE-03, SHARE-04, SHARE-05, SHARE-06, SHARE-07, SHARE-08, SHARE-09, SHARE-10
474	**Depends on:** Phase 14 (User-to-User Sharing), Phase 16 (Advanced Sync -- conflict resolution)
475	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
476	
477	1. Share entity supports permission levels (read/write) with default read for backward compatibility
478	2. Write-share recipients receive ECIES-wrapped IPNS private key alongside existing folder key
479	3. IPNS publish endpoint authorizes write-share recipients via shares table lookup
480	4. Owner can upgrade/downgrade share permission in-place via API endpoint
481	5. SharedFileBrowser shows [RW] badge, write toolbar, and full context menu for write shares
482	6. Write operations use withConflictRetry for multi-writer coordination (same as multi-device sync)
483	
484	**Plans:** 3/3 plans executed
485	
486	Plans:
487	
488	- [x] 27-01-PLAN.md -- Share entity/migration, DTOs, service methods, IPNS publish authorization expansion, API client regeneration
489	- [x] 27-02-PLAN.md -- Share store types, ShareDialog permission toggle, IPNS key wrapping, recipient permission management
490	- [x] 27-03-PLAN.md -- SharedFileBrowser write UI (badges, toolbar, context menu), useSharedNavigation write ops, 30s polling
491	
492	### Phase 36: Inline upload progress
493	
494	**Goal:** Replace the floating UploadModal popup with inline upload progress rows integrated directly into the file browser list, providing in-context upload feedback
495	**Requirements**: None (UI refactor, no formal requirement IDs)
496	**Depends on:** Phase 35
497	**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete
498	
499	Plans:
500	
501	- [x] 36-01-PLAN.md -- Upload store refactor to per-file tracking, upload loop migration to per-file actions
502	- [x] 36-02-PLAN.md -- UploadListItem component, FileList virtual entry merging, inline CSS, delete UploadModal/UploadItem
503	
504	### Phase 37: Parallel batch upload pipeline
505	
506	**Goal:** Replace sequential per-file upload loop with parallel encrypt+pin pipeline and single folder metadata update, reducing N folder IPNS publishes to 1 and enabling concurrent file processing
507	**Requirements**: D-01, D-02, D-03, D-04, D-05, D-06, D-07, D-08, D-09, D-10, D-11, D-12
508	**Depends on:** Phase 36
509	**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete
510	
511	Plans:
512	
513	- [x] 37-01-PLAN.md -- SDK batch uploadFiles() method with p-limit concurrency pool, stale-children re-read, partial failure handling
514	- [x] 37-02-PLAN.md -- Web Worker encryption offloading, EncryptionWorkerService wrapper, useDropUpload rewire to batch API
515	
516	---
517

519	_Last updated: 2026-03-30_
520	_Total M1.1 phases: 18 (18-35 complete) | Concern resolution: 5 phases_

Done. Here's a summary of what changed:

**Code Quality items pruned:**
- **DTS circular dependency** — removed. It's a devDependency in crypto, production dep in core. No runtime circular. By design.
- **useFileOperations.ts decomposition** — removed. 515 lines with 3 cohesive callbacks is reasonable. No benefit to splitting.

**Still valid (now Phase 38):**
- `folder.service.ts` — 1,059 lines, 9 importers still need migration to SDK
- `bin.service.ts` — 971 lines, but only 2 functions (`initializeBin`, `purgeExpired`) actually used

**New roadmap phases:**
- **Phase 38**: Retire deprecated web services (folder.service.ts + bin.service.ts)
- **Phase 39**: User-configurable vault parameters (bin retention as end-user setting)

> AGENT

The test imports `deriveRegistryIpnsKeypair` and `initializeVault` from `@cipherbox/core` to test domain separation between vault and registry IPNS derivation. The test verifies that crypto's `deriveVaultIpnsKeypair` produces different keys than core's `deriveRegistryIpnsKeypair`.

This is a test-only dependency. The test could be restructured — either moved to a cross-package integration test directory, or the comparison values could be hardcoded as test vectors instead of importing from core.

You're right — this was likely meant to be cleaned up after the restructuring. The devDependency can be removed if the test is refactored. It's a small change, and could easily be folded into Phase 38 since that's already about cleaning up the service layer. Want me to add it there, or just fix it now since it's a quick change?

### Summary

I've reviewed all 9 documentation files against the changes implemented in phases 28-37. Here are the findings:

### 1. **docs/ARCHITECTURE.md**

The note about Nitro as a fallback is outdated since Phala testnet TEE migration was completed. Consider updating to clarify this is now production-validated.

- **Line 261** (Section: Key Design Decisions #6): States "Nitro (planned fallback)" — should be updated to reflect Phase 35 completion with Phala CVM as the active implementation.

**Verification needed:** Line 277-281 references "Frozen Specifications" in `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/` — verify these paths still exist and are accurate.

### 9. **docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md**
**Last updated date in file:** 2026-03-24 (Line 4)  
**Status:** MOSTLY CURRENT with minor gaps

**Issues Found:**

### **Cross-Cutting Issues (Affecting Multiple Docs)**

1. **Phase 28 Impact Not Reflected**: Phase 28 (Code Hygiene & Logging) replaced all `console.*` calls with structured `logger.*`. None of the documentation files mention this change or reference the new logger module. This is not a breaking change, but documenting it (especially in DEVELOPMENT.md under "Code Quality") would be helpful.

2. **Phase 30 Impact Not Reflected**: Phase 30 (Web App Observability) added error tracking service and Grafana Faro integration. DEVELOPMENT.md and ARCHITECTURE.md do not mention observability setup. Consider adding a section to DEVELOPMENT.md about observability configuration.

3. **Phase 31 Impact Not Reflected**: Phase 31 (Structural Decomposition) split monolithic files. The ARCHITECTURE.md overview of packages/apps/crates structure is still accurate, but internal component decomposition is not documented.

4. **Phase 35 Impact Not Reflected**: Phase 35 (Phala Testnet TEE Migration) replaced the Docker simulator with real Phala CVM. ARCHITECTURE.md Line 18 mentions "Nitro fallback planned" but Phala is now the active implementation.

5. **Phase 37 Impact Not Reflected**: Phase 37 (Parallel Batch Upload Pipeline) added `uploadFiles()` batch method to SDK. The SDK documentation (in DEVELOPMENT.md and ARCHITECTURE.md) does not mention batch upload capability. This is important for users integrating the SDK.

---

1. **DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md**: Update Line 194 from "Phase 14" to "Phase 37". Verify and document any new migrations added in phases 28-37.

2. **ARCHITECTURE.md**: Update Lines 18 and 261 to replace "Nitro (planned fallback)" with "Phala testnet (Phase 35, active)".

3. **DEVELOPMENT.md**: Add a new "SDK Usage" section documenting the `uploadFiles()` batch method added in Phase 37.

**Medium Priority:**

5. **CAPACITY.md**: Add a note documenting when baselines were last captured and which phases are reflected.

6. **All docs**: Update "Last Updated" dates to reflect Phase 37 completion (2026-03-30).

**Low Priority:**

7. **DEVELOPMENT.md**: Add a note about structured logging (Phase 28) in the "Code Quality" section.

8. **METADATA_SCHEMAS.md**: Add cross-references to DeviceRegistry changes from Phase 24 (already documented, but could be clearer).

---

- **docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md** — No issues. Current and accurate.
- **docs/VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md** — No issues. Stable specification.
- **docs/METADATA_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md** — No issues. Comprehensive and current.

docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md:10:2. **macOS desktop** — FUSE-T virtual mount via SMB backend, accessed through Finder

docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md:25:**Rationale:** Original casing is always preserved in the encrypted metadata (`InodeData.name` / `FolderChild.name`). The normalization only affects HashMap key lookups in the FUSE layer. On macOS, NFC normalization prevents mismatches between composed and decomposed Unicode forms (e.g., `e` + combining acute vs. precomposed `e`). On Windows, lowercase folding implements the case-insensitive semantics that Explorer and all Windows applications expect.

docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md:156:| macOS    | SMB     | FUSE-T's SMB backend. NFS backend has unfixable kernel bug (WRITE RPCs never reach FUSE-T for new files). |
docs/FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md:157:| Windows  | WinFsp  | Native user-mode filesystem driver.                                                                       |

27	pub(crate) fn normalize_name(name: &str) -> String {
28	    // unicode-normalization is a dependency of the fuse feature.
29	    // On Windows (winfsp feature), fold to lowercase for case-insensitive matching.
30	    // WinFsp's case-insensitive lookup is the user-mode filesystem's responsibility.
31	    #[cfg(feature = "fuse")]
32	    {
33	        use unicode_normalization::UnicodeNormalization;
34	        name.nfc().collect()
35	    }
36	    #[cfg(all(feature = "winfsp", not(feature = "fuse")))]
37	    {
38	        name.to_lowercase()
39	    }
40	}
41

17	
18	### Case Handling
19	
20	| Platform | Storage    | Lookup                           | Implementation                                                |
21	| -------- | ---------- | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
22	| Web      | As-entered | Case-sensitive (`===`)           | `folder.service.ts`, `sdk-core/folder`                        |
23	| macOS    | As-entered | NFC-normalized                   | `inode.rs:normalize_name()` via `unicode-normalization` crate |
24	| Windows  | As-entered | Case-insensitive (lowercase key) | `inode.rs:normalize_name()` via `.to_lowercase()`             |
25	
26	**Rationale:** Original casing is always preserved in the encrypted metadata (`InodeData.name` / `FolderChild.name`). The normalization only affects HashMap key lookups in the FUSE layer. On macOS, NFC normalization prevents mismatches between composed and decomposed Unicode forms (e.g., `e` + combining acute vs. precomposed `e`). On Windows, lowercase folding implements the case-insensitive semantics that Explorer and all Windows applications expect.
```

```
1	# Architecture
2	
3	CipherBox implements layered, zero-knowledge encryption where **all user data is encrypted client-side before leaving the device**. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not plaintext file sizes (encrypted blob sizes are visible for storage accounting).
4	
5	## System Overview
6	
7	```text
8	User Device (Web/Desktop)
9	        ↓ Auth (4 methods)
10	CipherBox Backend (JWT)
11	        ↓
12	Web3Auth Network (Key Derivation)
13	        ↓ ECDSA Private Key (RAM only!)
14	User Device ← Vault Data ← PostgreSQL
15	        ↓ Encrypted Keys
16	IPFS (Kubo) ← Encrypted Files
17	        ↑
18	TEE (Phala; Nitro fallback planned) ← IPNS Republish (every 6h)
19	```
20	
21	**Key Properties:**
22	
23	- Same user identity → same keypair → same vault (auth methods share a vault when they resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`; see [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md))
24	- TEE republishes IPNS records even when all devices are offline
25	
26	## Key Derivation
27	
28	Two paths produce the same `VaultKey` (a secp256k1 keypair):
29	
30	```text
31	┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
32	│                       KEY DERIVATION                                │
33	├─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤
34	│  Web3Auth (Social Login)│  Web3Auth (External Wallet via SIWE)     │
35	│                         │                                           │
36	│  Google/Email/etc.      │  1. Wallet signs SIWE (EIP-4361) message │
37	│        ↓                │  2. Backend verifies SIWE, issues JWT    │
38	│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. Web3Auth Core Kit validates JWT      │
39	│        ↓                │  4. Web3Auth Network                     │
40	│  secp256k1 keypair      │        ↓                                  │
41	│  (deterministic)        │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │
42	│                         │                                           │
43	│                         │                                           │
44	├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤
45	│                              ↓                                      │
46	│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │
47	│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │
48	│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │
49	│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │
50	│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │
51	└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
52	```
53	
54	## Encryption Hierarchy
55	
56	Keys below the VaultKey are either **randomly generated** or **HKDF-derived**, then **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key. IPNS keypairs (vault, device-registry, per-file) are derived deterministically via HKDF-SHA256 from the user's private key (see [Cryptographic Primitives](#cryptographic-primitives)). Content encryption keys (rootFolderKey, per-folder keys, per-file keys) are randomly generated. Compromising one file key reveals nothing about other file keys.
57	
58	```text
59	    VaultKey (secp256k1 keypair)
60	    │
61	    │  ECIES-unwrap
62	    ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐
63	    │                                          │
64	    ▼                                          ▼
65	 rootFolderKey (random 32B)          rootIpnsPrivateKey (Ed25519, HKDF-derived)
66	    │                                          │
67	    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records
68	    ▼                                          ▼
69	 Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS publish/resolve
70	    │
71	    │  Contains per-child entries:
72	    │
73	    ├── File Pointer Entries ────────────────────────────────────────────┐
74	    │     name (encrypted)           ◄── only visible after              │
75	    │     timestamps (encrypted)         decrypting metadata             │
76	    │     fileMetaIpnsName (k51...)  ─────────────────────────────────────┤
77	    │          │                                                        │
78	    │          ▼                                                        │
79	    │     File Metadata (per-file IPNS record, encrypted JSON)          │
80	    │         fileKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ──► fileKey (32B)         │
81	    │         fileIv (12B)                             │                │
82	    │         cid (IPFS ref)          AES-256-GCM decrypt               │
83	    │                                                    ▼              │
84	    │                                             File Contents          │
85	    │                                                                   │
86	    ├── Subfolder Entries ──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
87	    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after                │
88	    │     timestamps (encrypted)      decrypting metadata               │
89	    │     folderKeyEncrypted ── ECIES-unwrap ──► folderKey              │
90	    │     ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ► ipnsKey              │
91	    │     ipnsName (k51...)                                             │
92	    │          │                                                        │
93	    │          ▼                                                        │
94	    │     Subfolder Metadata (same structure, recursive)                │
95	    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
96	```
97	
98	## Visibility Model
99	
100	```text
101	┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
102	│                    FULLY ENCRYPTED                               │
103	│   (requires user's private key + folder key to access)          │
104	│                                                                  │
105	│   ✓ File contents              ✓ File names                     │
106	│   ✓ Folder names               ✓ Folder structure / child list  │
107	│   ✓ File sizes                 ✓ Creation timestamps            │
108	│   ✓ Modification timestamps    ✓ All encryption keys            │
109	│   ✓ IPNS private keys          ✓ File-to-folder relationships   │
110	├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
111	│                    VISIBLE (Plaintext)                           │
112	│   (required for IPFS/IPNS protocol operation)                   │
113	│                                                                  │
114	│   • IPFS CIDs (content-addressed hashes, no semantic meaning)  │
115	│   • IPNS names (k51... public identifiers for folders)         │
116	│   • Encrypted blob sizes (approximate original sizes)          │
117	│   • Encryption IVs (required for decryption, not secret)       │
118	│   • User's secp256k1 public key                                │
119	├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
120	│                    NEVER STORED (RAM Only)                       │
121	│                                                                  │
122	│   • User's private key          • Decrypted file names          │
123	│   • Decrypted folder metadata   • Decrypted file contents       │
124	│   • Plaintext file/folder keys  • Wallet signatures             │
125	└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
126	```
127	
128	## Cryptographic Primitives
129	
130	| Purpose                             | Algorithm                  | Parameters                               |
131	| :---------------------------------- | :------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
132	| File & metadata encryption          | AES-256-GCM                | 256-bit key, 96-bit IV, 128-bit auth tag |
133	| Key wrapping                        | ECIES (secp256k1)          | Ephemeral keypair + AES-GCM              |
134	| Deterministic key derivation (IPNS) | HKDF-SHA256                | 32-byte output, context-specific salt    |
135	| IPNS record signing                 | Ed25519                    | 32-byte seed, 64-byte signatures         |
136	| Random generation                   | `crypto.getRandomValues()` | CSPRNG (Web Crypto API)                  |
137	
138	## Data Flows
139	
140	### File Upload
141	
142	```text
143	  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
144	  │                    FILE UPLOAD FLOW                           │
145	  │                                                              │
146	  │  1. User selects "document.pdf"                              │
147	  │         │                                                    │
148	  │         ▼                                                    │
149	  │  2. Generate random fileKey (32 bytes)                       │
150	  │     Generate random IV (12 bytes)                            │
151	  │         │                                                    │
152	  │         ▼                                                    │
153	  │  3. AES-256-GCM encrypt(plaintext, fileKey, IV)              │
154	  │     → ciphertext ‖ auth_tag (16 bytes)                       │
155	  │         │                                                    │
156	  │         ▼                                                    │
157	  │  4. ECIES wrap(fileKey, userPublicKey)                        │
158	  │     → ephemeral_pubkey ‖ wrapped_key ‖ tag                   │
159	  │         │                                                    │
160	  │         ▼                                                    │
161	  │  5. Clear plaintext fileKey from memory                      │
162	  │         │                                                    │
163	  │         ▼                                                    │
164	  │  6. Upload encrypted blob → IPFS (Kubo) → returns CID     │
165	  │         │                                                    │
166	  │         ▼                                                    │
167	  │  7. Create per-file FileMetadata entry (own IPNS record):    │
168	  │       { cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, ... }           │
169	  │         │                                                    │
170	  │         ▼                                                    │
171	  │  8. Add FilePointer to folder metadata children:             │
172	  │       { type: "file", nameEncrypted, nameIv,                 │
173	  │         fileMetaIpnsName, ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted? }         │
174	  │         │                                                    │
175	  │         ▼                                                    │
176	  │  9. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │
177	  │         │                                                    │
178	  │         ▼                                                    │
179	  │ 10. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │
180	  │         │                                                    │
181	  │         ▼                                                    │
182	  │ 11. Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │
183	  │     (signed with folder's Ed25519 private key)               │
184	  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
185	```
186	
187	## Defense in Depth
188	
189	Each file key is protected by multiple nested layers. An attacker must break through all layers to access any file content:
190	
191	```text
192	   File Content
193	     └─ encrypted with ──► fileKey (random, unique per file)
194	         └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key
195	             └─ stored inside ──► Folder Metadata
196	                 └─ encrypted with ──► folderKey (random, unique per folder)
197	                     └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key
198	                         └─ stored on ──► Server (zero-knowledge)
199	```
200	
201	## Threat Model
202	
203	With full access to IPFS and the CipherBox server but without the user's private key:
204	
205	```text
206	  IPFS (public network):
207	
208	    /ipfs/bafybei3a7x...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
209	    /ipfs/bafybei9f2k...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
210	    /ipfs/bafybeiqw8m...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
211	
212	  Without the user's private key:
213	    ✗ Cannot read file contents
214	    ✗ Cannot read file or folder names
215	    ✗ Cannot determine folder structure
216	    ✗ Cannot read timestamps or file sizes
217	    ✗ Cannot determine which blobs are files vs. folders
218	    ✓ Can see encrypted blob sizes (approximates original size)
219	    ✓ Can see IPNS update frequency (usage pattern)
220	```
221	
222	## Key Design Decisions
223	
224	### 1. Web3Auth for Key Derivation
225	
226	```text
227	Any auth method → CipherBox backend resolves userId → Web3Auth → Same ECDSA keypair
228	```
229	
230	### 2. Layered Encryption
231	
232	```text
233	File (AES-256-GCM) → Metadata (AES-256-GCM) → Keys (ECIES)
234	```
235	
236	### 3. Per-Folder IPNS
237	
238	```text
239	Root IPNS → Folder1 IPNS → Folder2 IPNS (modular sharing-ready)
240	```
241	
242	### 4. IPNS Polling Sync
243	
244	```text
245	30s polling, no push infrastructure (MVP simple)
246	```
247	
248	### 5. Zero-Knowledge Keys
249	
250	```text
251	Server holds: Encrypted root key only
252	Client holds: Private key (RAM only)
253	```
254	
255	### 6. TEE-Based IPNS Republishing
256	
257	```text
258	IPNS records expire after ~24h → backend scheduler triggers republish every 6h
259	Client encrypts ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key (ECIES)
260	TEE decrypts in hardware, signs, zeroes key immediately
261	Providers: Phala Cloud (current) / AWS Nitro (planned fallback)
262	```
263	
264	## User Journey Example
265	
266	```text
267	1. Signup (Google) → Web3Auth derives KeyA
268	2. Upload file → Encrypt → IPFS CID → IPNS publish
269	3. Phone login (Email) → Web3Auth derives KeyA (same!)
270	4. Phone polls IPNS → Sees file → Downloads & decrypts
271	5. Export vault → JSON with CIDs + encrypted root key
272	6. CipherBox gone? → Use export + private key → Full recovery
273	```
274	
275	## Further Reading
276	
277	- [Frozen Specifications](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/) — PRD, Technical Architecture, API Specification, Data Flows, Client Specification
278	- [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) — detailed auth flow documentation
279	- [Metadata Schemas](METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) — all metadata object schemas with field tables
280	- [Vault Export Format](VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md) — export/recovery data format
281	
```

192	## 6. Current Schema Reference
193	
194	12 tables as of Phase 14 (version 0.15.0):
195	
196	| #   | Table                     | Entity File                                 | Purpose                                   | Foreign Keys                              |
197	| --- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
198	| 1   | `users`                   | `auth/entities/user.entity.ts`              | User accounts (one per Web3Auth key)      | --                                        |
199	| 2   | `refresh_tokens`          | `auth/entities/refresh-token.entity.ts`     | JWT refresh token hashes                  | `userId` -> `users.id`                    |
200	| 3   | `auth_methods`            | `auth/entities/auth-method.entity.ts`       | Linked auth methods (wallet, email, etc.) | `userId` -> `users.id`                    |
201	| 4   | `vaults`                  | `vault/entities/vault.entity.ts`            | Encrypted vault root keys and IPNS names  | `owner_id` -> `users.id`                  |
202	| 5   | `pinned_cids`             | `vault/entities/pinned-cid.entity.ts`       | IPFS CID pin tracking for quota           | `user_id` -> `users.id`                   |
203	| 6   | `folder_ipns`             | `ipns/entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts`       | IPNS name -> CID mapping cache            | `user_id` -> `users.id`                   |
204	| 7   | `tee_key_state`           | `tee/tee-key-state.entity.ts`               | Current TEE key epoch (singleton)         | --                                        |
205	| 8   | `tee_key_rotation_log`    | `tee/tee-key-rotation-log.entity.ts`        | TEE key rotation audit log                | --                                        |
206	| 9   | `ipns_republish_schedule` | `republish/republish-schedule.entity.ts`    | IPNS auto-republish schedule              | `user_id` -> `users.id`                   |
207	| 10  | `shares`                  | `shares/entities/share.entity.ts`           | User-to-user share grants                 | `sharer_id`, `recipient_id` -> `users.id` |
208	| 11  | `share_keys`              | `shares/entities/share-key.entity.ts`       | Per-item encrypted keys for shares        | `share_id` -> `shares.id`                 |
209	| 12  | `device_approvals`        | `device-approval/device-approval.entity.ts` | MFA device approval requests              | -- (uses `user_id` varchar, not FK)       |
210	
211	All entity files are relative to `apps/api/src/`.
212	
213	**Note:** `device_approvals` uses `user_id` as a plain `varchar` rather than a foreign key to `users.id`. This is by design -- device approvals reference users by their Web3Auth identifier string, not the internal UUID. This table is not in the FullSchema baseline; it is created by its own incremental migration (`1740000000000-AddDeviceApprovals.ts`).

230	## 7. Environment Behavior Matrix
231	
232	| Aspect                      | Development                    | Test                           | Staging                        | Production                     |
233	| --------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ |
234	| `synchronize`               | `false`                        | `false`                        | `false`                        | `false`                        |
235	| Schema source               | Migration files (auto-run)     | Migration files (auto-run)     | Migration files only           | Migration files only           |
236	| Migration runner            | `migrationsRun: true` on start | `migrationsRun: true` on start | `run-migrations.ts` via Docker | `run-migrations.ts` via Docker |
237	| Missing migration visible?  | **Yes** -- table missing       | **Yes** -- table missing       | **Yes** -- deploy fails        | **Yes** -- deploy fails        |
238	| `migrations` table tracked? | Yes (determines what runs)     | Yes (determines what runs)     | Yes (determines what runs)     | Yes (determines what runs)     |
239	
240	**Configuration source:** `apps/api/src/app.module.ts`:
241	
242	```typescript
243	synchronize: false,
244	migrations: [join(__dirname, 'migrations', '*.js')],
```

```
245	migrationsRun: true,
246	```
247	
248	`synchronize` is **off in all environments**. Migrations auto-run on application start via `migrationsRun: true`. This ensures missing `CREATE TABLE` migrations surface immediately in dev/test rather than only failing on staging deploys.
249	
250	**One-time dev database reset:** After this change, existing dev databases created via `synchronize: true` may have tables not tracked in the `migrations` table. Reset with:
251	
252	```bash
253	dropdb cipherbox && createdb cipherbox
254	# Restart the API -- migrations will create all tables
255	pnpm --filter api dev
256	```
257	
258	**Convenience script:** `pnpm --filter api migrate:dev` runs pending migrations via the TypeORM CLI (useful for running migrations without starting the full app).
259	
260	**Migration runner (staging/production):** `apps/api/src/run-migrations.ts` -- standalone script that initializes a `DataSource` with `entities: ['dist/**/*.entity.js']` and `migrations: ['dist/migrations/*.js']`, calls `dataSource.runMigrations()`, and exits.
261	
262	**Deploy workflow:** `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml` runs:
263	
264	```bash
265	docker compose -f docker-compose.staging.yml run --rm \
266	  api node dist/run-migrations.js
267	```
268	
269	This executes before `docker compose up -d`, ensuring migrations complete before the application starts.
270	
271	---
272	
273	## 8. Dangerous Patterns
274	
275	### 8.1 `synchronize: true` Masking Missing Migrations (RESOLVED)
276	
277	**The problem:** Previously, `synchronize: true` in dev/test caused TypeORM to auto-create tables from entity decorators. A developer could add `@Entity('shares')`, the table would appear, tests would pass, and the PR would ship -- all without writing a migration. The missing `CREATE TABLE` only surfaced when the staging deploy failed.
278	
279	**The fix:** `synchronize` is now `false` in all environments. Migrations auto-run via `migrationsRun: true`. If a developer adds a new entity without a migration, the dev server fails immediately with `relation does not exist`. Additionally, every PR that adds a new `@Entity()` must include a `CREATE TABLE` migration. Code reviewers must check for this. See Section 4.3.
280	
281	### 8.2 Migration Timestamp Ordering
282	
283	**The problem:** `1740300000000-SharesPartialUniqueIndex.ts` modifies the `shares` table. If a developer creates the `shares` table in migration `1740400000000` (a later timestamp), TypeORM runs the index modification first -- on a table that doesn't exist yet.
284	
285	**The fix:** `CREATE TABLE` migrations must have the earliest timestamp among all migrations that touch the same table. When adding a new table, verify no existing migration references it.
286	
287	### 8.3 `down()` Ownership Conflicts
288	
289	**The problem:** Both `FullSchema.ts` and `AddSharesTables.ts` create the `shares` table. If you revert `AddSharesTables` on a database where `FullSchema` created the table, you drop a table you don't "own".
290	
291	**The fix:** `down()` should use `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` and include a comment explaining the ownership ambiguity. The `AddSharesTables.ts` migration does this correctly:
292	
293	```typescript
294	public async down(queryRunner: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {
295	  // On staging/production this migration created both tables, so dropping them here is correct.
296	  // On fresh databases, FullSchema (1700000000000) created these tables and this migration
297	  // only added indexes. Reverting only this migration on a fresh DB will drop FullSchema-owned
298	  // tables; ensure FullSchema is also reverted to maintain consistency.
299	  await queryRunner.query(`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS "share_keys" CASCADE`);
300	  await queryRunner.query(`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS "shares" CASCADE`);
301	}
302	```
303	
304	### 8.4 Non-Idempotent DDL
305	
306	**The problem:** `CREATE TABLE` without `IF NOT EXISTS` fails on databases where `synchronize: true` already created the table (e.g., if a staging database was previously run in dev mode, or if FullSchema already created the table).
307	
308	**The fix:** Always use `IF NOT EXISTS` for `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, and column-existence checks for `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`. PostgreSQL does not support `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` before version 9.6, but CipherBox targets PostgreSQL 15+.
309	
310	---
311	
312	## 9. Historical Incidents
313	
314	### 9.1 Phase 14: Missing `shares` and `share_keys` Tables (PR #186)
315	
316	**Date:** 2026-02-21
317	**Severity:** Staging deploy failure
318	**Root cause:** Phase 14 added `Share` and `ShareKey` entities with `@Entity()` decorators, plus a migration (`1740300000000-SharesPartialUniqueIndex`) that modified the `shares` table's unique constraint. But no migration created the `shares` or `share_keys` tables themselves. In dev/test, `synchronize: true` auto-created them. On staging, the partial unique index migration failed with `relation "shares" does not exist`.
319	
320	**Resolution:**
321	
322	1. Created `1740250000000-AddSharesTables.ts` with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` for both tables (timestamp before the index migration)
323	2. Documented the incident in `.learnings/` and added the TypeORM migration discipline rule to `MEMORY.md`
324	3. Note: FullSchema was **not** updated — it is a point-in-time snapshot (see Guiding Principle 4 and Section 9.2)
325	
326	**Lesson:** `synchronize: true` in dev/test is invisible safety net that masks missing migrations. Every new `@Entity()` requires a corresponding `CREATE TABLE` migration.
327	
328	### 9.2 Clarification: FullSchema Does Not Need Every Table
329	
330	**Date:** 2026-02-22
331	
332	During the creation of this protocol, the `device_approvals` table was identified as missing from `FullSchema.ts`. Initial analysis suggested this was a gap, but on review: FullSchema is a point-in-time baseline, not a living document. Fresh database deployments run FullSchema first, then all incremental migrations. The `1740000000000-AddDeviceApprovals.ts` migration uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and runs on both fresh and existing databases. There is no need to duplicate the DDL into FullSchema.
333

20	- **Encryption** — Client-side AES-256-GCM for files and metadata. ECIES secp256k1 key wrapping (per-file, per-folder random keys). AES-256-CTR streaming for large media files. Web Worker offloading for batch encryption.
21	- **File Management** — Upload, download, rename, move, delete. Batch upload pipeline with concurrent encryption and pinning. Nested folders up to 20 levels. Drag-and-drop. File versioning with point-in-time restore (up to 10 versions per file).
22	- **Sharing** — User-to-user sharing with ECIES re-wrapping (read-only and read-write). Invite-link sharing with time-limited access tokens. Multi-recipient sharing. Lazy key rotation on revoke.
23	- **Recycle Bin** — 30-day soft-delete with restore and permanent delete.
24	- **Search** — Client-side encrypted search index across file and folder names. Fuzzy matching with Cmd/Ctrl+K shortcut.
25	- **Media Preview** — Image, PDF, video (streaming CTR decryption), and audio preview.
26	- **Sync** — Multi-device via IPNS polling (~30s). Conflict detection and resolution (sequence-number based).
27	- **Desktop** — macOS and Windows via Tauri v2 + FUSE-T/WinFsp virtual filesystem mount. Background sync with system tray. SMB backend on macOS.
28	- **TEE Republishing** — Automatic IPNS record refresh every 6 hours via Phala Cloud. Zero-knowledge: keys decrypted only inside hardware enclaves.
29	- **Data Portability** — Full vault export (JSON + encrypted blobs). Standalone recovery with private key — no CipherBox required.
```

91:**Rationale:** 100 MB balances usability with memory constraints. The entire file must fit in memory for AES-256-GCM encryption (authenticated encryption requires the full plaintext). AES-256-CTR streaming mode could support larger files but is currently only used for decryption/playback, not upload. The Web Worker encryption offload helps with CPU blocking but does not change the memory requirement.
173:| AES-256-CTR | Large media streaming (decryption) | > 256 KB (video)    | Enables streaming playback without full decrypt |
```

CTR mode is used only for decryption/playback of large media files where streaming is essential for UX. Uploads always use GCM. The mode is recorded in file metadata so the correct decryption path is selected on download.",
  "new_string": "**Rationale:** GCM provides authentication (tamper detection) but requires the entire plaintext in memory. CTR mode is used for large media files (video, audio) both on upload and download, enabling streaming without buffering the entire file. The encryption mode is recorded in file metadata so the correct decryption path is selected on download."
}

fixed frozen specs path\n- **docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md** — added 6 missing migrations, 2 new tables (share_invites, pin_migrations), updated from 12 to 14 tables\n- **CLAUDE.md** — fixed stale `00-Preliminary-R&D/` paths, TEE description, republish interval\n\n### Planning updates\n- **.planning/DEFERRED.md** — thorough audit removing 10+ implemented/irrelevant items (Grafana Faro, session replay, FUSE CTR streaming, Linux FUSE, vault v1 columns, batch pin warnings, per-file IPNS conflict detection, bin retention env var, DTS circular dep, useFileOperations decomposition); There's the problem: **"ECIES decryption failed for all available epochs"**.

The TEE CVM is running and healthy, but it can't decrypt the IPNS private keys that were encrypted for it. This means the TEE public key that clients used to encrypt IPNS keys doesn't match the key the CVM is deriving.

This likely happened because the CVM was redeployed (Phase 35 migration) and derived a new key, but existing enrollments still have keys wrapped with the old TEE public key.

1	# CipherBox Filesystem Specification
2	
3	This document defines the rules, constraints, and rationale governing the CipherBox virtual filesystem. These rules apply across all platforms (web, desktop macOS, desktop Linux, desktop Windows) and are enforced at different layers depending on the operation.
4	
5	## Design Principles
6	
7	CipherBox must present a consistent filesystem experience across three very different access surfaces:
8	
9	1. **Web browser** — drag-and-drop uploads, folder tree UI
10	2. **macOS desktop** — FUSE-T virtual mount via SMB backend, accessed through Finder and Terminal
11	3. **Linux desktop** — kernel FUSE (libfuse3) virtual mount, accessed through file manager and terminal
12	4. **Windows desktop** — WinFsp virtual mount, accessed through Explorer, PowerShell, and Git Bash
13	
14	The strictest platform constraint wins. Windows is case-insensitive and has the most restricted filename rules, so CipherBox defaults to rules compatible with Windows even when accessed from other platforms. This ensures files created on any platform are accessible on every other platform.
15	
16	## Naming Rules
```

145	### Content Download
146	
147	| Parameter        | Value  | Constant                   |
148	| ---------------- | ------ | -------------------------- |
149	| Download timeout | 120s   | `CONTENT_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT` |
150	| Block size       | 4096 B | `BLOCK_SIZE`               |
151	
152	**Rationale:** File content is fetched from IPFS on `open()` and cached in memory. Large files (up to 100 MB) can take 30-60 seconds from the staging IPFS gateway. The 120-second timeout accommodates slow networks while preventing indefinite hangs. After the initial fetch, all `read()` calls are served from cache.
153	
154	### Mount Backend
155	
156	| Platform | Backend | Notes                                                                                                     |
157	| -------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
158	| macOS    | SMB     | FUSE-T's SMB backend. NFS backend has unfixable kernel bug (WRITE RPCs never reach FUSE-T for new files). |
159	| Linux    | libfuse | Kernel FUSE via libfuse3. Standard FUSE semantics, no translation layer.                                  |
160	| Windows  | WinFsp  | Native user-mode filesystem driver.                                                                       |
161	
162	### Inode Management
163	
164	- **Root inode:** Always `1` (standard FUSE convention)
```

225	**@phala/dstack-sdk:**
226	
227	- Risk: Phala-specific SDK for CVM key derivation. Tightly coupled to Phala Cloud infrastructure. No alternative provider is implemented.
228	- Impact: If Phala Cloud has an outage or breaking API change, the TEE republishing pipeline stops. No fallback means IPNS records eventually expire (72-hour TTL).
229	- Migration plan: Abstract key derivation behind an interface so alternative providers (AWS Nitro Enclaves, Azure Confidential Computing) can be plugged in. Currently blocked by the complexity of re-implementing key derivation + epoch management for a second provider.
230	
231	## Missing Critical Features
232	
233	**No offline support (web or desktop):**
